## No. 850-D <note>The Sanskrit letter 'Gha' (घ) serves as a classification marker, equivalent to the fourth letter 'D' in English archival systems.</note>
## **<term>The Ancient Treatise of Garga</term>** (**<term>Astral Science</term>**) <note>original: वृद्धगार्गी (Vṛddhagārgī). 'Vṛddha' translates to 'elder' or 'ancient,' and Garga is a revered Vedic sage. <term>Jyotiṣam</term> is the traditional Indian discipline encompassing astronomy and astrology.</note>

# The Compendium of the Elder Garga

Om. Welfare! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha. <note>original: "Om svasti Śrī-Gaṇeśāya namaḥ"</note> Om. Salutations to Saraswati, the goddess of learning. <note>original: "Om namaḥ Sarasvatyai"</note>

Om. With palms joined in a gesture of reverence, the sage Narada <note>A celestial sage known as the messenger of the gods</note> asks the Elder Garga—the greatest among the twice-born, whose brilliance is equal to the sun:

**The Holy Narada said:**

"Tell me the true names and natures of the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras: the 27 or 28 sectors of the sky through which the Moon passes</term>. How many types of **Planets** <term>Grahas</term> are there, and what are the **Secondary Planets** <term>Upagrahas</term>? Explain the determination of the stars and the characteristics of the **Auspicious Conjunctions** <term>Yogas</term>.

Explain the division of the zodiac signs and what the **Ascendants** <term>Lagnas: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> are. Speak also of the teachings of the sage Atri, the knowledge of the **Auspicious Timing** <term>Bhadra-karana</term>, the use of fried grains in rituals, the seasons, and the **Auspicious Fixity** <term>Vaidhriti</term>.

In the chapter regarding marriage, let nothing beneficial remain unsaid. All of this should be spoken with great effort solely for the benefit of humanity. This essence of the principles of Astrology is a collection of truths. Just as the soul is the essence of the person, or the Himalayas are the essence of the mountains, so is Astrology the essence of all wisdom.

Even the deeds performed in previous lives—whether good or bad—remain present in this life. It is seen that even **Janaki** <note>A name for Sita, the heroine of the Ramayana</note>, for whom the great sage Vasishtha himself selected the **Ascendant** <note>The astrologically "perfect" moment for her wedding</note>, still had to experience great suffering. I wish to hear the essence of how our actions interact with the stars; tell me, O best of sages!"

Having heard the words of Narada, the Elder Garga spoke:

**The Elder Garga said:**

"Listen, Narada, to the true determination of the Lunar Mansions. I shall explain them to you in sequence and with brevity. 

There are 27 Lunar Mansions (or 28 when including the intercalary mansion, **Abhijit**). 

They are: 
1. **Ashvini** 
2. **Bharani** 
3. **Krittika** 
4. **Rohini** 
5. **Mrigashira** 
6. **Aridra** 
7. **Punarvasu** 
8. **Tishya** <note>Also known as Pushya</note> 
9. **Ashlesha** 
10. **Magha** 
11. **Purva-phalguni** 
12. **Uttara-phalguni** 
13. **Hasta** 
14. **Chitra** 
15. **Svati** 
16. **Vishakha** 
17. **Anuradha**

<summary>This page initiates a dialogue where the sage Narada asks the Elder Garga to explain the fundamental principles of astrology, acknowledging that while stars influence life, destiny (karma) remains a powerful force. It concludes with the beginning of a formal list of the 28 lunar mansions.</summary>
<keywords>Narada, Garga, Nakshatra, Jyotisha, Karma, Janaki, Sita, Vasishtha, Astrology, Lunar Mansions</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical classification of the heavens initiated by the sage Garga in response to Narada’s request. The previous page ended with the first half of the 27 lunar mansions (Nakshatras); this page completes that list and begins the systematic pairing of celestial bodies with their presiding deities and the categorization of time.</meta>

...Jyeshtha, Mula, Purva-Ashadha, Uttara-Ashadha, Abhijit, <note>Abhijit is the "intercalary" or 28th mansion, often omitted in later 27-mansion systems but vital in ancient Vedic astrology.</note> Shravana, Dhanishtha, Shatabhishak, Purva-Bhadrapada, Uttara-Bhadrapada, and Revati.

## **The Deities of the Mansions**

The Ashvins <note>Twin physicians of the gods</note> for Ashvini; Yama <note>Lord of Death</note> for Bharani; Agni <note>the Fire God</note> for Krittika; Prajapati <note>the Creator</note> for Rohini; Soma <note>the Moon God</note> for Mrigashirsha; Rudra <note>the Howler/Shiva</note> for Ardra; Aditi <note>Mother of the Gods</note> for Punarvasu; Brihaspati <note>Priest of the Gods</note> for Tishya; <note>original: "Tiṣyāḥ", an older name for the mansion Pushya.</note> the Serpents for Ashlesha; and the Ancestors <note>original: "Pitaraśca"</note> for Magha.

Aryaman for Phalguni; <note>Purva-Phalguni</note> Aryaman for Uttara-Phalguni; Savitr <note>the Solar deity</note> for Hasta; Tvashtr <note>the Divine Architect</note> for Chitra; Vayu <note>the Wind God</note> for Svati; Indra and Agni for Vishakha; Mitra <note>the Friend/God of Alliances</note> for Anuradha; Indra for Jyeshtha; Nirriti <note>the Goddess of Dissolution</note> for Mula; the Waters for Purva-Ashadha; the Universal Gods <note>original: "Viśvedevā"</note> for Uttara-Ashadha; Brahma for Abhijit; Vishnu for Shravana; the Vasus <note>eight elemental deities</note> for Dhanishtha; Varuna <note>God of the Oceans</note> for Shatabhishak; Aja-Ekapada <note>the One-Footed Goat, a celestial storm deity</note> for Purva-Bhadrapada; Ahirbudhnya <note>the Serpent of the Deep</note> for Uttara-Bhadrapada; and Pushan <note>the Nourisher</note> for Revati.

## **The Planets**

The Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn.

## **The Secondary Planets**
**4**
Rahu, <note>the North Node of the Moon, responsible for eclipses</note> Ketu, <note>the South Node of the Moon or comets</note> Dhruva, <note>the Pole Star</note> and Agastya. <note>the star Canopus</note>

## **The Deities of the Planets**

Agni is the deity of the Sun; in the Vedas, Varuna is spoken of as the deity of the Moon; Kumara <note>Skanda, the god of war</note> is for the Son of Earth <note>Mars</note>; Vishnu is spoken of for Mercury; Indra is for Jupiter; Saraswati is for Venus; Prajapati is for Saturn; then Ganapati is for Rahu; Rudra is for Ketu; Dhruva is the deity of the Fixed Star; and Ananta <note>the Infinite Serpent</note> is for the Infinite. These are proclaimed as the deities of the planets.

## **The Lunar Days**

Nanda <term>The Joyful: 1st, 6th, and 11th days</term>, Bhadra <term>The Auspicious: 2nd, 7th, and 12th days</term>, Jaya <term>The Victorious: 3rd, 8th, and 13th days</term>, Rikta <term>The Empty: 4th, 9th, and 14th days</term>, and Purna <term>The Full: 5th, 10th, and 15th days</term>.

On a Nanda day, all works may be undertaken; on a Bhadra day, misfortune may occur; on a Jaya day, victory is assured; on a Rikta day, it bestows inauspiciousness; and on a Purna day, all welfare is attained. Such is the word of the sage Garga.

## **The Stars**

Birth, Prosperity-bestowing... <note>This begins the 'Tara' system where the 27 Nakshatras are categorized based on their relation to one's birth star.</note>

<summary>This page lists the presiding deities for the 28 Nakshatras and the primary and secondary planets. It also introduces the "Tithi" (Lunar Day) classification system and their general astrological effects as taught by Garga.</summary>
<keywords>Nakshatra Deities, Nine Planets (Grahas), Secondary Planets (Upagrahas), Tithi classification, Garga’s teachings, Indian Astrology (Jyotisha)</keywords>

# The Stars of Misfortune, Security, Opposition, Achievement, Destruction, Friendship, and Great Friendship

| | | | | | | | | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

<insert>
Now, regarding the system of Stars and the Birth Mansion, this should be observed with great diligence.
</insert>

The Results: The first, tenth, and nineteenth stars from one’s own are the **Birth** <note>original: "Janma"</note> stars. In this combination, all actions result in "fruitless activity"; primarily, worldly endeavors should be avoided. However, other [routine] actions will succeed; of this, there is no doubt.

The eleventh, second, and twentieth stars possess the quality of **Prosperity** <note>original: "Sampat-kara"</note>. One should know that in "Prosperity," the successful completion of work arises.

The third, twenty-first, and twelfth stars are the **Misfortune-makers** <note>original: "Vipat-kara"</note>. Know that in "Misfortune," the destruction of one's work will occur.

**Security** <note>original: "Kshema"</note> is the fourth, twenty-second, and thirteenth star. In the "Security" star, all forms of success are born.

Then, the fifth, twenty-third, and fifteenth <note>The text says "thirteenth" earlier and "fifteenth" here; standard systems usually count the 5th, 14th, and 23rd</note> are the **Obstacle** <note>original: "Pratyari", transcribed here as "Pratyahari"</note> stars. It is said that in these stars, a loss of wealth will surely occur.

The sixth, fifteenth, and twenty-fourth stars are the **Achievers** <note>original: "Sadhaka"</note>. In "Achievement," whatever work is undertaken results in the fulfillment of its purpose.

The seventh, sixteenth, and twenty-fifth stars thereafter are the **Destructive** <note>original: "Naidhana"</note> ones. In "Destruction," one must consider that ruin will surely happen.

One should know the seventeenth, twenty-sixth, and eighth stars as **Friendship** <note>original: "Maitra"</note>. If the commencement of a task is undertaken in "Friendship," [it is supported].

In the **Great Friendship** <note>original: "Parama-maitra"</note> stars—the twenty-seventh, eighteenth, and ninth—all activities are fruitful.

The Birth, Prosperity, Security, Achievement, Friendship, and Great Friendship stars are the ones that accomplish the purpose of all deeds. The Misfortune, Obstacle, and Destructive stars—the third, fifth, and seventh in the sequence—cause the destruction of all aims; one who knows this should avoid them.

When Venus <note>original: "Shukre"</note> is in the Birth star, there is severe suffering. When Jupiter <note>original: "Jive"</note> is in Prosperity...

<vocab>
<term>Vipat-tara (Misfortune Star): The 3rd, 12th, and 21st stars from the birth star, considered unlucky.</term>
<term>Kshema (Security): A favorable star indicating comfort and safety.</term>
<term>Naidhana (Destruction): The 7th star from the birth star, often associated with danger or endings.</term>
<term>Nakshatra (Lunar Mansion): The 27 segments of the ecliptic through which the moon passes.</term>
</vocab>

<summary>This page outlines the Navatara (Nine Star) astrological system, which categorizes the 27 lunar mansions into nine groups to predict the outcome of actions based on a person's birth star.</summary>
<keywords>Navatara, Janma Nakshatra, Electional Astrology, Jyotisha, Nakshatra classification, Sanskrit manuscript</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the sequence of the "Navatara" or nine-star system of classification begun on the previous page. Having covered the first five categories (Birth, Prosperity, Misfortune, Security, and Obstacle), it concludes with the remaining four categories before moving into a new section regarding specific "Yogas" (celestial combinations or unions) and their auspicious or inauspicious results.</meta>

...the sixth, fifteenth, and twenty-fourth stars from one’s birth mansion are the **Accomplishment** <note>original: "Sadhaka"</note> stars. These are generally favorable. If these fall on a Tuesday <note>original: "Bhauma," the day of Mars</note>, loss occurs; however, through the daily influence of Saturn <note>original: "Sanaischara"</note>, these stars are considered excellent for practitioners. The seventh group <note>The seventh, sixteenth, and twenty-fifth stars</note> is known as **Destruction** <note>original: "Vadha"</note> and is said to destroy wealth and the king's favor. The eighth is **Friendship** <note>original: "Maitra"</note> and the ninth is **Great Friendship** <note>original: "Parama-maitra"</note>, which follow the Moon's influence. These lead to fire, fear, travel, and the gain of women or fine beds in their respective order.

### Now, the Yogas <term>Yogas: specific mathematical combinations of the Sun and Moon that determine the "flavor" or quality of a day</term>

The twenty-eight Yogas are: **Bliss** (Ananda), **Rod of Time** (Kaladanda), **Smoke** (Dhuma), **Lord of Creatures** (Prajapatya), **Creator** (Dhata), **Banner** (Dhvaja), **Beloved of Fortune** (Srivatsa), **Thunderbolt** (Vajra), **Mallet** (Mudgara), **Parasol** (Chatra), **Friendly** (Maitra), **Mental** (Manasa), **Desirable** (Kamya), **Conversation** (Alapaka), **Uprooting** (Unmula), **Death** (Mrityu), **One-Eyed** (Kana), **Perfected** (Siddha), **Auspicious** (Shubham), **Nectar** (Amritam), **Elephant** (Gaja), **Pestle** (Mushala), **Great Elephant** (Matanga), **Decay** (Kshaya), **Moving** (Chara), **Fixed** (Sthira), and **Increasing** (Pravardha).

### Now, the Results of the Yogas

In **Bliss** <note>original: "Ananda"</note>, there shall be the gain of wealth. In **Night of Time** <note>original: "Kalaratri", likely synonymous with Kaladanda</note>, there is great fear. In **Smoky** <note>original: "Dhaumya"</note>, there shall be prosperity of wealth. In the **Lord of Creatures** <note>original: "Prajapatya"</note>, there is the happiness of offspring. In the **Gentle** <note>original: "Saumya"</note>, there is success in all actions. In the **Creator** <note>original: "Dhata"</note>, one should assign the gain of physical goods. In the **Banner** <note>original: "Dhvaja"</note>, there is the arrival of clothing, knowledge, women, leather, and gems. 

In the **Thunderbolt** <note>original: "Vajre"</note>, there is fear of lightning and weapons. In the **Mallet** <note>original: "Mudgare"</note>, there is fear of death. In the **Parasol** <note>original: "Chatre"</note>, there is prosperity of all goals. In the **Friendly** <note>original: "Maitri"</note>, there is a meeting with friends. In the **Mental** <note>original: "Manase"</note>, one should know the gain of a wife. In **Desire** <note>original: "Kame"</note>, the attainment of one's wishes. In **Conversation** <note>original: "Alapake"</note>, there shall be gain. In **Uprooting** <note>original: "Unmule"</note>, there is the destruction of wealth. 

At the time of **Death** <note>original: "Mrityu"</note>, one encounters death. In the **One-Eyed** <note>original: "Kane"</note>, there is the destruction of limbs. The **Perfected** <note>original: "Siddha"</note> combination is proclaimed to bring success in all purposeful actions. In the **Auspicious** <note>original: "Shubha"</note>, fear of disease is abandoned. In the one called **Nectar** <note>original: "Amrita"</note>, there is the destruction of enemies. In the **Elephant** <note>original: "Gaja"</note>, there is splendor and gems. In the **Pestle** <note>original: "Mushala"</note>, the arrival of quarrels. In the **Great Elephant** <note>original: "Matanga"</note>, the obtaining of a tusker. In **Decay** <note>original: "Kshaya"</note>, there will be sickness. In the **Moving** <note>original: "Chara"</note>, one should perform activities involving travel. In the **Fixed** <note>original: "Sthira"</note>, stability increases. In the **Increasing** <note>original: "Pravardhamana"</note>, there is always a continuous growth of grain.

### Now, the Vishkumbha and Other Yogas

Taking the position where the Sun stands, starting from the star Ashvini, and where the Moon stands, joining them together and subtracting one... <unclear>1</unclear>

It then provides specific weekday-star combinations that form specialized Yogas like the Nectar Yoga and the Birth Yoga.</meta>

3
</margin>

The [Nitya] Yogas are: **Obstacle** <note>original: "Vishkumbha"</note>, **Affection** <note>original: "Priti"</note>, **Long-lived** <note>original: "Ayushman"</note>, **Good Fortune** <note>original: "Saubhagya"</note>, **Splendid** <note>original: "Sobhana"</note>, **Great Throat/Danger** <note>original: "Atiganda"</note>, **Good Doer** <note>original: "Sukarma"</note>, **Constancy** <note>original: "Dhriti"</note>, and **Spear/Pain** <note>original: "Shula"</note>.
Also **Joint** <note>original: "Ganda"</note>, **Growth** <note>original: "Vriddhi"</note>, **Fixed** <note>original: "Dhruva"</note>, **Blow/Obstruction** <note>original: "Vyaghata"</note>, **Joy** <note>original: "Harshana"</note>, **Thunderbolt** <note>original: "Vajra"</note>, **Success** <note>original: "Siddhi"</note>, **Calamity** <note>original: "Vyatipata"</note>, **Excellent** <note>original: "Variyan"</note>, **Iron Bar/Obstacle** <note>original: "Parigha"</note>, **Auspicious** <note>original: "Shiva"</note>, **Success** <note>original: "Siddhi"</note>, **Feasible** <note>original: "Sadhya"</note>,
**Favorable** <note>original: "Shubha"</note>, **Bright/White** <note>original: "Shukla"</note>, **Creator/Immense** <note>original: "Brahma"</note>, **Chief** <note>original: "Aindra"</note>, and **Unsteady** <note>original: "Vaidhriti"</note>. These Yogas are declared to grant results according to their names <note>meaning favorable names bring favorable outcomes, and vice-versa</note>. ॥ **Now, the Bliss Yoga** <note>original: "Ananda-yoga"</note> ॥
The [Bliss Yoga] occurs when the star **Ashwini** falls on a Sunday <note>original: "Aditya-vara"</note>, **Mrigashirsha** on a Monday <note>original: "Soma"</note>, and the eighth star [Pushya] on a Friday <note>original: "Bhargava-agre"</note>. It is not considered when the star **Hasta** falls on a Wednesday <note>original: "Budha"</note>. [It also occurs with] **Anuradha** on a Thursday <note>original: "Guru"</note>, **Uttara Phalguni** on a Friday <note>original: "Shukra"</note>, and **Shatabhishak** on a Saturday <note>original: "Saurivara"</note>; thus they are well-declared. ॥ **Now, the Attraction Yoga** <note>original: "Karsha-yoga"</note> ॥ When the Moon is in **Pushya**, or when the group of stars beginning with **Hasta** occurs, or **Vishakha**... or **Dhanishta** and **Revati** <note>original: "Paushnam"</note> when it is a Wednesday, and **Saubhagya** <note>the 4th Nitya Yoga</note> falls on a Saturday—one should certainly perform various types of ritual actions, self-study, and recitation. However, one should avoid travel, experimental actions, buying, and selling, as well as marriage. Indeed, one should only perform divine rituals. ॥ **Now, the Nectar Yoga** <note>original: "Amrita-yoga"</note> ॥ [When the star] **Hasta** falls on a Sunday, **Rohini** on a Monday, **Mula** on a Tuesday <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, **Revati** on a Wednesday, and when it is a Thursday <note>original: "Jiva"</note> with its specific portion, these are known as the **Nectar** [Yogas]. ॥ **Now, the Perfected Yoga** <note>original: "Siddha-yoga"</note> ॥ The stars **Shravana** <note>original: "Vaishnava"</note>, **Chitra** <note>original: "Tamra"</note>, and **Mrigashirsha** <note>original: "Saumya"</note> are declared [to form this yoga] on the day of the Moon [Monday]. One should perform actions during this declared **Perfected Yoga**. ॥ **Now, the Birth Yoga** <note>original: "Janma-yoga"</note> ॥ **Bharani** on a Sunday, **Chitra** on a Monday, **Uttara Phalguni** on a Thursday, **Jyeshtha** on a Friday, and **Revati** on a Saturday—these are declared to be the **Birth Yogas**. One should not perform a marriage during these Birth stars. In marriages, it leads to widowhood, and in travel, death will occur.

<summary>This page lists the twenty-seven daily Nitya Yogas and provides the specific weekday-star combinations that constitute the Bliss, Nectar, Perfected, and Birth Yogas, noting which are auspicious for rituals and which are dangerous for marriage or travel.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the catalog of specialized astrological Yogas (celestial combinations). While the previous page listed the 27 Nitya Yogas and began the Bliss Yogas, this page transitions into more specific combinations of weekdays, lunar days (Tithis), and lunar mansions (Nakshatras) that either guarantee success or portend disaster. It specifically details the "Burned," "Saw," and "Destruction" combinations that practitioners must avoid for major life events.</meta>

<margin>
Vr. Da.
Ga.
3
3
</margin>

In trade, actions are rendered fruitless, and service also becomes without reward. One should not perform any auspicious act during these combinations when they coincide with one’s birth stars <note>original: "prajanm-rkshesu"; the lunar mansion the moon was in at the time of one's birth</note>. 

The combination of the Sun <note>implied by the sequence</note> with **Roots** <note>original: "Mula"</note>, **Hearing** <note>original: "Shravana"</note>, or **Elder** <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note>; Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> with **Later Happy Foot** <note>original: "Uttara Bhadrapada"</note>; Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> with its own **The Cutters** <note>original: "Krittika"</note>; Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> with **Return of the Wealthy** <note>original: "Punarvasu"</note>—these grant all fruits. Additionally, Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> with **The Independent** <note>original: "Swati"</note> or **The Friendly** <note>original: "Anuradha/Maitra"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note> with **The Friendly** <note>original: "Anuradha"</note> are known as **Perfect Combinations** <note>original: "Siddha Yoga"</note>; they grant the successful completion of all works.

The Sun with **The Mighty** <note>original: "Magha/Matta"</note>; the Moon with **The Branched** <note>original: "Vishakha"</note>; Mars with **The Nourisher** <note>original: "Pushya"</note>; Mercury with **Roots** <note>original: "Mula"</note>; Jupiter with **The Hundred Physicians** <note>original: "Shatabhisha/Varuna"</note>; Venus with **The Red One** <note>original: "Rohini"</note>; and Saturn with **The Ancestors** <note>original: "Magha/Pitre"</note>—all these are declared by the Sages to be **All-Destroying Combinations** <note>original: "Sarva-ghata Yoga"</note>. 

In the same way, the following are unlucky: [Sunday with the twelfth], Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> on the second lunar day; the Son of the Moon <note>term: "Somasuta" — Mercury</note> on the third; Jupiter on the fourth; Saturn on the fifth; the Moon on the thirteenth; and the Sun <note>original: "Bhaskara"</note> on the fourteenth. These are always remembered as being like fire. || **Thus ends the Burned Lunar Day Yoga** <note>original: "Tithi-dagdha-yoga"</note> ||

On the sixth lunar day if it is Saturn's day; on the seventh if it is Venus's <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> day; on the eighth if it is the Minister of the Gods' <note>term: "Deva-mantri" — Jupiter</note> day; on the ninth if it is Mercury's day; on the tenth if it is Mars's <note>original: "Bhaumavara"</note> day; and on the eleventh if it is the Moon's <note>original: "Shashanka"</note> day—one should avoid these days as their rays are sinful. The results of these combinations are like being cut by a saw, in due order. || **Thus ends the Saw Combination** <note>original: "Krakacha-yoga"</note> ||

On the seventh lunar day if it is the Sun's day; on the first <note>original: "Pratipada"</note> if it is Mercury's; on the eleventh if it is the Moon's; on the ninth if it is the Son of the Earth's <note>term: "Bhusuta" — Mars</note>; on the tenth if it is Saturn's <note>original: "Shaure"</note>; on the twelfth <note>original: "Vaivasvati"</note> if it is the Sun's; and on the thirteenth if it is Venus's—this combination is known as **The Destroyer** <note>original: "Samvarta"</note>. It causes the destruction of wisdom and means, as well as death and disease. **Thus ends the Destruction Combination.** ||

Marriage, travel, gathering wealth, and auspicious rites for cattle should be avoided here. In marriages [performed during these times], widowhood surely follows.

<summary>This page lists specific weekday, lunar day (Tithi), and star (Nakshatra) combinations, categorizing them into auspicious "Siddha" Yogas and inauspicious "Dagdha," "Krakacha," and "Samvarta" Yogas. These calculations are intended to warn the user against performing major life events like marriage or business during dangerous celestial alignments.</summary>

In travel, death may occur; in the ritual of entering a new home <term>Grihapravesha; the ceremony performed when first inhabiting a house</term>, if the rites are performed without proper invitation or procedure, they will go toward fruitlessness. The three stars of **The Branched** <note>original: "Vishakha"</note>, **The Mother of Gods** <note>original: "Aditi/Punarvasu"</note>, and **The Creator** <note>original: "Dhata/Rohini"</note>; the three of the East; the three of **The Wealthiest** <note>original: "Dhanishtha"</note> on a Tuesday <note>original: "Bhaume"</note>; the three of the "Time-Keepers" <note>original: "Varavati"</note> on a Wednesday <note>original: "Budhe"</note>; **The Red One** <note>original: "Rohini"</note> and **The Horsemen** <note>original: "Ashvini"</note> on a Thursday <note>original: "Jive"</note>; the three of **The Nourisher** <note>original: "Pushya"</note> on a Friday <note>original: "Shukre"</note>; and the three of **The Companion** <note>original: "Aryaman/Uttara Phalguni"</note> on a Saturday <note>original: "Saure"</note>—these must be avoided with great effort. These combinations <term>Yogas</term> are declared to bring contrary and harmful results.

### Concerning Other Traditions
In other traditions, it is said: From the star of **The Flower** <note>original: "Pushya"</note> onwards, or from the stars of Jupiter and the Sun, one should calculate the order of the combinations. There will be loss, destruction, imprisonment, or gain depending on the alignment. In the case of unfavorable **Nourisher** <note>original: "Pushya"</note> combinations, one must avoid three ghatikas <note>A ghatika is a traditional unit of time lasting 24 minutes; three ghatikas equal 72 minutes</note>. For those residing in the soil <note>perhaps referring to foundation-laying</note>, avoid five, six, or seven measures of time. One should also avoid the "Dog Combination" <note>original: "Shuno yoga"; a specific malefic astrological alignment</note> whenever it occurs. Avoid the Sun with **The Friendly** <note>original: "Anuradha"</note> or **The Universal Gods** <note>original: "Vaishvadeva/Uttara Ashadha"</note>; Saturn with **The Hundred Physicians** <note>original: "Shatabhishak"</note>; Mars with the first stars; Jupiter with **The Horsemen** <note>original: "Ashvini"</note> or **The Mighty** <note>original: "Magha"</note>; or the three stars of Jupiter on a Wednesday; the **Serpent** <note>original: "Ashlesha"</note> on a Friday; and the Sun’s son <note>Saturn</note> with **The Mighty** <note>original: "Magha"</note> or **The Hand** <note>original: "Hasta"</note>. These combinations are considered inauspicious.

### The Zodiac Signs
**The Ram** <note>original: "Mesha" (Aries)</note>; **The Bull** <note>original: "Vrisha" (Taurus)</note>; **The Twins** <note>original: "Mithuna" (Gemini)</note>; **The Crab** <note>original: "Karkata" (Cancer)</note>; **The Lion** <note>original: "Simha" (Leo)</note>; **The Maiden** <note>original: "Kanya" (Virgo)</note>; **The Scales** <note>original: "Tula" (Libra)</note>; **The Scorpion** <note>original: "Vrishchika" (Scorpio)</note>; **The Archer** <note>original: "Dhanvi" (Sagittarius)</note>; **The Sea-Monster** <note>original: "Makara" (Capricorn)</note>; **The Water-Bearer** <note>original: "Kumbha" (Aquarius)</note>; **The Fish** <note>original: "Meena" (Pisces)</note>.

**The Fish** and **The Ram** have a duration of three ghatikas <note>1 hour 12 minutes</note>. **The Bull** and **The Water-Bearer** consist of four. **The Sea-Monster** and **The Twins** are five. The others are traditionally known to be six ghatikas. **The Crab** and **The Archer** are six; **The Scorpion** and **The Lion** are six; **The Scales** and **The Maiden** are also six, according to the measurement of the rising sign <term>Lagna</term>. During the day, **The Fish** and **The Ram** are two <note>unclear units, likely a variation in calculation</note>; **The Bull** and **The Water-Bearer** are seven and a half; **The Sea-Monster** and **The Twins** are six; the remaining signs do not move in this manner. In other sources: **The Fish** and **The Ram** are three ghatikas... <unclear>portion regarding calculations for Brahmins and ritual impurity</unclear> ...fifteen units...

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical and astrological data following the warnings about ritual timing. The previous page concluded by advising practitioners to avoid certain star combinations for travel or house-warming rituals. This page transitions into the formal definitions of the twelve houses (Bhavas) of a horoscope and the relationship between the lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and the solar zodiac signs (Rashis), providing the fundamental building blocks for constructing a birth chart.</meta>

In the Bow <note>original: "Capaka" referring to Sagittarius</note> and in the five-parts, the Bull <note>original: "Vrisha" referring to Taurus</note> is five measures <term>Nalika; a measure of time roughly equal to 24 minutes</term>, the Lion <note>original: "Simha" referring to Leo</note> is six measures, and the Ram <note>original: "Mesha" referring to Aries</note> is four measures. The Maiden <note>original: "Kanya" referring to Virgo</note> is one; in the Scales <note>original: "Tula" referring to Libra</note> and the Bull, five measures are recorded.

### **Now, the Names of the Houses**
The Body <gloss>1</gloss>, Wealth <gloss>2</gloss>, Siblings <gloss>3</gloss>, Friends <gloss>4</gloss>, Children <gloss>5</gloss>, Enemies <gloss>6</gloss>, Spouse <gloss>7</gloss>, Death <gloss>8</gloss>, Virtue <gloss>9</gloss>, Profession <gloss>10</gloss>, Gains <gloss>11</gloss>, and Losses <gloss>12</gloss>.

<note>These twelve categories, or "Bhavas," represent the different areas of human life governed by the twelve sections of a horoscope.</note>

In whichever zodiac sign the Sun is situated, the wise man should determine the day and night. Six signs in the sky are declared for the day, and six for the night. In the Horned Goat <note>original: "Makara" referring to Capricorn</note> there is an increase of two and six; in [the signs starting with] the Ram <note>original: "Mesha" referring to Aries</note>, four and five; in the Fish <note>original: "Mina" referring to Pisces</note> and the Ram, three; in the Bull, four; and in the Twins <note>original: "Mithuna" referring to Gemini</note>, ten are said to be the measures of the day. In the Crab <note>original: "Karkata" referring to Cancer</note>, these measures; in the Lion, four and five; six in the Maiden. In the Scales, six; in the Scorpion <note>original: "Vrischika"</note>, three; in the Bow <note>original: "Dhanus" referring to Sagittarius</note>, a loss of day-length is declared.

### **Concerning the Arrangement of Signs**
The stars of **The Horsemen** <note>original: "Ashvini"</note> and **The Bearer** <note>original: "Bharani"</note>, and the first part of **The Cutter** <note>original: "Krittika"</note>, belong to the Ram [Aries], the sign of **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>. 

The remaining three parts of **The Cutter**, **The Red One** <note>original: "Rohini"</note>, and half of **The Deer's Head** <note>original: "Mrigashira"</note>, belong to the Bull [Taurus], the sign of **Venus** <note>original: "Bhargava"</note>. 

The other half of **The Deer's Head**, **The Moist One** <note>original: "Ardra"</note>, and three parts of **The Return of Wealth** <note>original: "Punarvasu"</note>, belong to the Twins [Gemini], the sign of **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note>. 

The remaining one part of **The Return of Wealth**, **The Nourisher** <note>original: "Tishya/Pushya"</note>, and **The Embracer** <note>original: "Ashlesha"</note>, belong to the Crab [Cancer], the sign of the **Moon** <note>original: "Chandra"</note>. 

**The Mighty** <note>original: "Magha"</note>, **The Former Reddish One** <note>original: "Purva Phalguni"</note>, and one part of **The Latter Reddish One** <note>original: "Uttara Phalguni"</note>, belong to the Lion [Leo], the sign of the **Sun** <note>original: "Surya"</note>. 

The remaining three parts of **The Latter Reddish One**, **The Hand** <note>original: "Hasta"</note>, and half of **The Bright One** <note>original: "Chitra"</note>, belong to the Maiden [Virgo], the sign of **Mercury**. 

The other half of **The Bright One**, **The Independent One** <note>original: "Svati"</note>, and three parts of **The Branched** <note>original: "Vishakha"</note>, belong to the Scales [Libra], the sign of **Venus**. 

The remaining one part of **The Branched**, **The Small Flower** <note>original: "Anuradha"</note>, and **The Eldest** <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note>, belong to the Scorpion [Scorpio], the sign of **Mars**. 

**The Root** <note>original: "Mula"</note>, **The Former Invincible One** <note>original: "Purva Ashadha"</note>, and one part of **The Latter Invincible One** <note>original: "Uttara Ashadha"</note>, belong to the Bow [Sagittarius], the sign of **Jupiter** <note>original: "Devaguru"</note>. 

The remaining three parts of **The Latter Invincible One**, **The Ear** <note>original: "Shravana"</note>, and half of **The Wealthiest** <note>original: "Dhanishtha"</note>, belong to the Horned Goat [Capricorn], the sign of **Saturn** <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note>. 

The other half of **The Wealthiest**, **The Hundred Physicians** <note>original: "Shatabhisha"</note>, and three parts of **The Former Lucky Feet** <note>original: "Purva Bhadrapada"</note>, belong to the Water-Pot [Aquarius], the sign of **Saturn** <note>original: "Saura"</note>. 

The remaining one part of **The Former Lucky Feet**, **The Latter Lucky Feet** <note>original: "Uttara Bhadrapada"</note>, and **The Wealthy** <note>original: "Revati"</note>, belong to the Fish [Pisces], the sign of **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jiva"</note>. 

Two and a quarter stars <note>original: "Sapaada-dvaya-nakshatra"</note> are utilized for each sign.

### **Thus ends the section on signs.**

...Of that, the result is the increase of wealth, sons, grandsons, and so on. In the relationship of the sixth and eighth signs <note>original: "shadashtaka"; a challenging astrological alignment where two signs are six and eight houses apart</note>, death, enmity, separation, and disease are produced from the death-dealing position. In the trine <note>original: "trikona"; the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</note>, supreme love is declared. In the seventh-house alignment <note>original: "samasaptaka"; where signs are directly opposite each other</note> as well, the great sages have declared a powerful bond of affection. Even in the case of a sixth-eighth relationship, if one observes a state of friendship between the lords of the signs or a single lordship <note>original: "ekadhipatyam"; when both zodiac signs are ruled by the same planet</note> over the signs, it brings auspiciousness to men at the time of marriage, provided the stars <note>original: "tara"; the lunar mansions</note> are mutually compatible. 

|| Thus ends the **Consideration of the Zodiac Signs** <note>original: "Rashi Vichara"</note> || 

### <term>Now, the Consideration of Marriage</term> <note>original: "Atha Vivaha Vichara"; a technical section focusing on the timing and conditions for a wedding</note>

In the month of Shravana <note>roughly July–August</note>, one is always miserable... In Ashvayuja <note>the month of Ashvin, September–October</note>, there is no love. In Kartika <note>October–November</note>, sons and beauty are attained. One obtains wealth and a sweet-speaking wife in Margashirsha <note>November–December</note>. In the month of Pausha <note>December–January</note>, she becomes a widow or childless, without a doubt. In Magha <note>January–February</note>, she is endowed with discipline and rules, and in Phalguna <note>February–March</note>, she is devoted to her husband.

<margin>
Vr̥. Ḍa.
Mā.
9
</margin>

...in the month of Caitra <note>roughly March–April</note>, she becomes husbandless. In the fifth [lunar day]... In Vaiśākha <note>April–May</note>, there is abundant love. In Jyeṣṭha <note>May–June</note>, she becomes a virtuous woman with sons. In Āṣāḍha <note>June–July</note>, she is miserable and does not live on [proper] food.

### **Now, the constellations for marriage** <note>original: "Athavivāha nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

The three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara-phalguni, Uttara-ashadha, and Uttara-bhadrapada</note>, Saumya <note>another name for the star Mrigashira</note>, Rohini, and Shravana; as well as Hasta, Mula, Magha, Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>, and Paushna <note>Revati</note> are remembered as [the stars] for marriage.

**Moreover** <note>original: "Anyacca"</note>

Hasta, Mula, Magha, Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>, Revati, Rohini, Punarvasu, Vayu <note>Svati</note>, Mriga <note>Mrigashira</note>, Ashvini, and Bhanu <note>Shravana</note>—these eleven are for marriage.

**Moreover**

[Marriage] in Mrigashira, Magha, Hasta, Maitra <note>Anuradha</note>, and the "Uttara" stars is not condemned for childbirth. In Paushna <note>Revati</note> and the "Divine" stars <note>referring to a category of nakshatras</note>, it is said she will be endowed with wealth, fortune, and many servants, bringing satisfaction to her kinsmen. In Āṣāḍha, there are sons... according to the rules... in Agneya <note>Krittika</note> it is desired... the rest result in a mixture of auspicious and inauspicious results, such as anger, illness, or death...

### **Now, the ascendants for marriage** <note>original: "Atha vivāha lagnāni"; the "lagna" is the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ceremony</note>

In Kriya <note>the sign of Aries</note>, the maiden's son... without color or beauty... but if she is married into a different lineage <note>original: "vigotratā"</note>, she brings joy to both families. In the third [house/sign], she does not [possess] much wealth... in Hari <note>Leo</note>, she is pleasant and once she has taken refuge [in the husband's home], she is a favorite of both her father and her husband, and obtains wealth from her father-in-law's family. In Tula <note>Libra</note>, she is unsteady and without a sword... In Dhanu <note>Sagittarius</note>, she is of noble birth... in the end, she attains a state of non-aging...

### **Now, the chapter on marriage** <note>original: "Atha vivāha paṭalaṃ"</note>

The Sun in the first house <note>the "Tanū" or body house</note> makes her a widow. The Moon... causes misfortune for the children... the lord of the night <note>the Moon</note> causes the destruction of the wife. 1. <unclear>r̥ṣai</unclear>... by the Sun, no kingdom; by Mars, turmeric [is lost/affected]...

...the rising sign <note>the *Lagna* or the zodiac sign on the eastern horizon</note> determines her character.

### **The Results of the Houses** <note>original: "Bhava-phala"</note>

**The Second House**
A woman of pure intellect, if Jupiter and Venus are balanced in the second house, certainly becomes a mistress of wisdom. She gives birth to sons and attains great fame. **2**

**The Third House**
If the Moon, Mars, Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury are in the third house, the woman gives birth to many children and becomes a sharer of great wealth. However, if the Moon and Saturn <note>literally: "the Sun's son"</note> are in their own astrological portions here, it makes her pleasure-loving but certainly harsh or wicked in her habits. **3**

**The Fourth House**
In the fourth house, if planets are in their own occupations <note>meaning in their own signs or well-placed</note>, they may grant a short life or misfortune if malefic planets <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally seen as "harsh"</note> are present. However, Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus in this position grant happiness and the joy of children. **4**

**The Fifth House**
If the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> or Mars—the "harsh ones"—are in the fifth house, there is a loss of children. If Jupiter and Venus <note>literally: "Bhargava"</note> are there, they grant great royal-like favor and many sons. Saturn brings severe disease, while the Moon <note>referred to as "Yashanka," the one marked with glory</note> causes the birth of incomparable daughters. **5**

**The Sixth House**
When Saturn, the Sun, and Jupiter are placed in the sixth house, the woman becomes very virtuous and is endowed with many religious duties. The Moon in this position causes widowhood, and Mars <note>literally: "the son of the earth"</note> brings great disease. If Mercury is here, she will have many children and be the beloved of her husband. **6**

**The Seventh House**
If Saturn, the Sun, Jupiter, Mars, Mercury, and Venus are placed in the seventh house—especially if they are malefic—it results in the following [misfortunes] in order: the fault of widowhood, being tormented by harm, the destruction of wealth, disease, travel to foreign lands, and death. **7**

**The Eighth House**
In the eighth house, if the lord of that house and Jupiter are present, separation is certain. Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> causes a woman to become a widow. Mars brings great disease. However, Saturn <note>the Sun’s son</note> here makes her wealthy and the beloved of her husband. **8**

**The Ninth House**
In the house of religion <note>Dharma</note>, if Saturn, the Sun, and Mars <note>son of the earth</note> are placed, she is devoted to her duties. If Mercury and Jupiter are there, she is free from disease. These planets grant royal sons, while the Moon <note>Yashanka</note> causes the birth of incomparable daughters. **9**

**The Tenth House**
When Rahu is in the tenth house <note>literally: "the sky-surface"</note>, it makes her a widow. If the Sun is there, being devoted to sin, again...

</margin>

If Mercury and Mars <note>original: "Marun-matsa" and "Kuja"</note> are placed together in the tenth house, the woman becomes the mistress of a wealthy household and is exceedingly fortunate. **10** In the eleventh house <note>the house of gains and income</note>, the Sun, Jupiter, and Mercury bring various rewards. If Saturn <note>literally: "the son of the Sun"</note> is here, he brings great wealth. The Moon <note>indicated by the unclear "titti"</note> <unclear>tittim</unclear> <unclear>titum</unclear>... the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> brings wealth and various fruits. If the Moon is present here, she may become a widow or possess the arts of a deer-eyed woman. **11** In the twelfth house <note>the house of loss and expenditure</note>, Jupiter makes her wealthy, but the Sun <note>referred to as "the maker of day"</note> makes her poor. If Mercury <note>the "knower"</note> is here, she will spend her wealth excessively. If the sign of the Lion <note>Leo</note> is here, she may be born of a wicked family. Venus <note>Bhargo</note> makes her a virtuous woman <note>a "Sadhvi"</note>, while Mercury <note>the son of the Moon</note> grants her both wealth and sons. Saturn <note>the son of the Sun</note> makes her heart cruel and harsh. 

In the fifth house <note>the house of children</note>, if malefic planets <note>traditionally "harsh" planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are present, she may lose her children or remain childless. One should avoid certain placements in the ascendant <note>the Lagna</note>... all planets in the ascendant are equal to Jupiter if the Moon and others are positioned in the five centers <note>Kendras</note>. 

**Now, Saturn** <note>original: "Atha Shanih"</note>

If Saturn is in the constellations of Indra or Vishakha, it may bring death. If in a gentle constellation, he makes her humble and endowed with children. If the Moon is joined with a limited measure <note>possibly a specific degree or debilitation</note>, she may take up the life of a wandering ascetic <note>Pravrajya</note>. If Saturn and the Moon are together, or if Mercury and Venus are in the proximity, she may suffer from confusion of the mind or diseases of the intellect. If malefic planets are in the house of children, it brings widowhood, sorrow, and empty efforts.

**Now, the Seventh House** <note>original: "Atha Jamitram"; Jamitra is a technical term for the house of marriage</note>

If the Moon is in the seventh house along with the Sun, the woman will be devoid of good character. If Mars is in the seventh, she causes the death of her husband. If the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> is there, she becomes a relative to many but lacks a stable home. If Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> is in the seventh, she remains devoted to her husband. If Ketu <note>the South Node</note> is there, she is seen as possessing wealth and good family ties. If Mercury and Venus <note>the son of Bhrigu</note> are in the seventh, she is the beloved of her husband, highly virtuous, and a noble lady.

This has been told by the Sage for the desire of benefiting mankind. Having determined these truths from the essence of the teachings, one should perform journeys, marriages, house-building, consecrations, and the sacred thread ceremony <note>Upanayana</note>. One should choose an auspicious ascendant and avoid an unfavorable time, considering the final results of the Moon and the gains brought by the Moon.

**Thus ends the Chapter on Marriage.** <note>original: "Iti Vivahapatale"</note>

<meta>This page transitions from the general planetary influences on a woman's life to the specific calculations of marital compatibility (Melapak). It begins with residual instructions on ritual timing and then moves into the classification of the twenty-seven Nakshatras (lunar mansions) into three Ganas (temperaments) and various Yonis (animal archetypes), which are foundational for determining the harmony between a couple.</meta>

Suffering, little splendor, and loss of wealth; or health and the fulfillment of all desires—these are the results of bathing <note>the text refers to "Snana," ritual purification</note> in the sequence beginning from the Monkey <note>referring to a specific astrological timing or transition</note>. By bathing during the transitions of the Sun, on the Sun's day <note>Sunday</note>, Friday, or Thursday, and specifically during the conjunctions of the eighth, sixth, and thirteenth lunar days, one avoids aging and decay. 

Ashwini, Krittika <note>original: "Anala"</note>, Punarvasu <note>original: "Aditya"</note>, Hasta, Pushya <note>original: "Haloma"</note>, Mrigashira, Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note>, Shravana, and Revati—these are known as the **Divine Ganas** <term>Deva Gana: a temperament classification associated with refinement, kindness, and spiritual inclination</term>. 

Bharani, Rohini, Ardra <note>original: "Indra"</note>, and the three "Prior" stars <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>—these are proclaimed by the sages most learned in the scriptures as the **Human Ganas** <term>Manushya Gana: a temperament associated with ambition, sociability, and earthly pursuits</term>. 

Krittika, Magha, Ashlesha, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Vishakha, Mula, Dhanishta <note>original: "Panishtha"</note>, and Shatabhisha <note>original: "Varuna"</note>—these are characterized as the **Demonic Ganas** <term>Rakshasa Gana: a temperament associated with intensity, dominance, and strong-willed nature</term>. 

Affection is best between two Divine types; it is medium between a Divine and a Human type. **Now** regarding the Divine and Demonic, or the Human and Demonic: if a Demonic woman and a Human man <unclear>marry</unclear>, it is considered a medium match. However, the scriptures say that <note>certain combinations</note> may cause death. When the woman’s temperament and the man’s temperament are the same, the affection is excellent; therefore, one should strive to marry within compatible temperaments. 

**Now** regarding the **Animal Archetypes** <term>Yoni: a classification system based on animal behaviors to determine physical and instinctive compatibility</term>. 

The Dog and the Cow are assigned to Krittika and <unclear>Pushya</unclear>; the Elephant is Rohini; the Horse is Mula; the Dog is Shravana; the Rat is Magha and Purva Phalguni. Hasta and Swati are the Buffalo; the Cow species is found among three others. The Tiger is Vishakha and Chitra; the Deer is Jyeshtha and Anuradha. The Monkey is Punarvasu; the Elephant is Revati and Bharani. The Goat <note>original: "Vasta"</note> is Uttara Bhadrapada; the Lion is Ashwini; and the Horse is Shatabhisha <note>original: "Varuna"</note>. The Snake and the Cat are also among these animal types. 

One should pair those who are mutually strong. There is natural affection between the Snake and the Monkey, and similarly between the Buffalo and the Cow. The Lion and the Tiger share a bond, as does the Rat group. Conversely, just as the Dog and the Elephant are natural enemies, so too is the enmity between the Elephant and the Lion, or the Cat...

It establishes a hierarchy of "best," "medium," and "avoidable" matches based on these instinctive and behavioral traits.</summary>

The compatibility between the Mouse and the Dog, the Cat and the Mouse, the Sheep and the Monkey, the pair of Snakes, the Buffalo and the Camel, the Cow and the Tiger, and the pair of Dogs—these should be examined with great care <note>this refers to the Yoni-vairam, or natural enmities between animal archetypes in Vedic astrology, which would suggest incompatibility in marriage</note>.

**Now, the Auspicious Stars for Shaving**

Rohini, the three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini <note>original: "Prajapatya"</note>, Jyeshtha <note>original: "Aindra"</note>, Shatabhisha <note>original: "Varuna"</note>, and Swati <note>original: "Vayavya"</note> are considered excellent for the ritual of shaving <term>Kshaura: the ritual cutting of hair or beard, performed for purification or as part of a rite of passage</term>.

**In Another Text**

Shravana, Dhanishta, Mrigashira, Chitra, Revati, Hasta, Punarvasu, and Pushya—these nine are auspicious for the shaving rite. <unclear>Shaving at the tip of the hand...</unclear> Shaving on Friday increases sorrow. 
<margin>The Sun and Moon</margin> 
Tuesday destroys the entire body; Saturn likewise. Shaving should be performed specifically on the days of Mercury <note>Wednesday</note> and the Moon <note>Monday</note>. On the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note>, one should avoid the ritual of shaving, the meal <note>associated with the ritual</note>, oil massage, and sexual union.

The **Fixed** <note>Dhruva</note> stars are four: Rohini <note>original: "Prajapatya"</note> and the three "Latter" stars. 
The **Soft** <note>Mridu</note> stars are Mrigashira <note>original: "Saumya"</note>, Chitra, Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note>, and Revati. 
The **Swift** <note>Kshipra/Laghu</note> stars are Hasta, Pushya, and Ashwini. 
The **Movable** <note>Chara</note> stars are Punarvasu <note>original: "Aditya"</note>, Swati <note>original: "Vayavya"</note>, Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha <note>original: "Varuna"</note>. 
The **Dreadful** <note>Daruna/Ugra</note> stars are Ashlesha, Mula, Magha, Bharani, and the three "Prior" stars <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>. 
Krittika and Vishakha are known as the **Mixed** <note>Sadharana</note> stars.

The wise determine the performance of rituals based on these definitions. Under Soft, Swift, and Movable stars, one should perform shaving, the first tonsure <term>Chuda: a child's first ritual haircut</term>, the initiation ceremony <term>Upanayana: the sacred thread ceremony</term>, taking religious vows, and the ritual gift of a cow. Harvesting, gathering crops, and the commencement of agriculture should also be done under these stars, as well as any other auspicious work. <unclear>Regarding the weapon and the bow...</unclear> under Vishakha and the specific days, one should perform Movable or Fixed activities, pacification rituals, and rites for nourishment.

## **Now, the Stars for Wearing New Clothes**

Wearing clothes for the first time under the star **Krittika** <note>original: "krittika"; the Pleiades</note> leads to the garment being burnt by fire. In **Rohini**, it brings wealth and prosperity. Under the **Deer’s Head** star <note>original: "mrigashira"</note>, one will obtain delicious food. In **Ardra**, death may occur. **Punarvasu** is auspicious for prosperity, and **Pushya** <note>original: "tishya"</note> bestows gold.

In **Ashlesha**, the garment will be consumed by fire, and in **Magha**, it leads to death itself. The **Former Reddish Star** <note>original: "phalguni-anta," referring to Purva Phalguni</note> results in seizure by the King <note>this likely refers to legal trouble or confiscation of property</note>, while the **Latter Reddish Star** <note>original: "uttara phalguni"</note> grants gold. The **Hand** star <note>original: "hasta"</note> is known to provide enjoyment, and **Chitra** brings knowledge.

**Swati** brings the sound of sweet food and drink; **Vishakha** makes one pleasant to look upon. **Anuradha** <note>original: "dhanya-prada nara ha cha"</note> provides grains, but **Jyeshtha** destroys the garment. Under **Mula**, the garment is <unclear>lost</unclear>; all manner of obstacles always haunt the clothing. The **Latter Victorious** star <note>original: "uttara ashadha"</note> is said to grant more clothes, but **Shravana** causes eye disease.

**Dhanishta** leads to the gain of wealth. In **Shatabhisha**, there is <unclear>danger from water</unclear>. 
<margin>The letter 'Va' twice</margin> 
<gloss>The letter 'Va' indicates loss</gloss> 
...gives clothes. In **Revati**, there is a gain of wealth, but in **Ashvini**, death itself. In **Bharani**, a multitude of diseases will persist for as long as the garment is worn.

Dhanishta, Revati, Pushya, Punarvasu, Ashvini, Rohini, and Hasta—these are the stars for quickly obtaining <unclear>royal favor</unclear> and clothes.

## **Now, the Results of the Days of the Week**

On **Sunday**, the garment is unstable and constantly damp. On **Tuesday**, there is fear of fire and thieves. On **Wednesday**, the garment provides happiness. On **Thursday**, there is an increase in one's wardrobe. On **Friday**, success <note>original: "akah"</note> begins. On **Saturday**, fear of disease lasts for as long as the garment is worn. Red garments and the initial taking of garments should be avoided <note>on certain days</note>. In marriages or in the presence of a King, the rules for garments are relaxed as per common agreement.

## **Now, the Piercing of the Ears**

Revati, Shravana, Hasta, Dhanishta, Mrigashira, and Pushya—these are the stars for the procedure of piercing the ears <term>Karna-vedhanam: a traditional rite of passage where a child's ears are pierced for health and decoration</term> in children, as the great sages proclaim.

## **Now, the Stars for Travel**

The three "Latter" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Mrigashira <note>original: "saumyam"</note>, Rohini, Shravana, and Anuradha <note>original: "maitram"</note>...

<margin>
Brihat Samhita
Verse 59
</margin>

->The stars **Hasta**, **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, **Ashwini**, **Chitra**, **Agni** <note>original: "agni"; referring to the star Krittika</note>, **Anila** <note>original: "anila"; referring to the star Swati</note>, and the three **Uttaras** <note>the three "latter" stars: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> are declared suitable for **Vastu** <note>the science of architecture and building placement</note>. Elsewhere, it is said:<-

->One should carry out architectural works under **Shravana**, **Uttara Ashadha**, **Magha**, **Swati**, **Revati**, **Ashwini**, and **Pushya**. Construction should be avoided on the day of **Krittika**. One should not begin building if the moon is in the seventeenth or tenth <unclear>mansion</unclear> from the birth star; in the middle or at the face of the house, it leads to loss, whereas the three types of enjoyment and wealth are gained otherwise.<-

->**Now, the Ascendants for Architecture**<-

->The signs of **Taurus**, **Gemini**, **Virgo**, **Libra**, and **Sagittarius** are mobile <note>referring to Chara or "moving" signs, though some listed here are fixed or dual; the text suggests specific qualities for these signs</note>; in these six, installation is firm. One should never perform these rites under **Scorpio**. Because of their stability, **Leo** and **Aquarius** are recommended, but not **Scorpio**. If the Moon and the stars are in the center <term>Kendra|the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</term> or the eighth or the houses of increase <term>Upachaya|the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, where planets tend to give better results over time</term>, and the malefic planets are in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, it is considered auspicious. When the Moon is in its soft or latter half, and the benefics are in the centers or triangles <term>Trikona|the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</term>, and the malefics have reached the third or sixth houses, the **Fixing of the Pillar** <note>original: "Shankusthapana"; the ritual installation of the first pillar or gnomon to determine orientation</note> is desired.<-

->When the malefics have gone to the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, and the Ascendant is in a center house, the **Vastu** is firm and free from calamities. At the start of all works, for the results to be fruitful, one should consider the birth star of the builder. In one's own village or in a stable house, the **Entrance into the House** <note>original: "Grihapravesha"</note> or the habitation should be performed. Mars, even if situated in the center, the twelfth, or the third house, grants stability.<-

->The head of the **Vastu Person** <note>original: "Vastunara" or Vastu Purusha; the presiding deity of a building site, envisioned as a man lying on the ground</note> is traditionally placed in the **Northeast** <note>original: "Ishanakona"</note>, and the feet are most auspicious in the **Southwest** <note>original: "Nairrityabhaga"</note>. The arms are situated in the **Southeast** and **Northwest** corners. This is how the Vastu Person is described. In the month of **Margashirsha** <note>approx. November–December</note>, the head is in the east. In the third month, **Phalguna** <note>approx. February–March</note>, it is in the south. In the three months beginning with **Jyeshtha** <note>approx. May–June</note>, the head is in the west. Building during these times kills the mother and father; building in **Pushya** <note>approx. December–January</note> leads to loss of wealth and the death of sons.<-

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Vastu Shastra (architecture) from the previous page, moving from specific star signs to the effects of the lunar months and days of the week on construction. It then transitions into the specific rituals for Deva Pratishtha (the installation of deity images) and the timing for Prasthana (setting out on journeys).</meta>

If construction begins under the star **Pushya**, fire will cause disease and pain; however, others say that in the practice of **Vastu** <note>the traditional science of architecture</note>, it brings the gain of wealth and property. 

The results of beginning construction in the various lunar months are as follows:
*   In **Magha**, fire leads to destruction. 
*   In **Phalguna**, the site becomes blessed with good fortune. 
*   In **Chaitra**, it brings joy to the people. 
*   In **Vaishakha**, there is a depletion of wealth. 
*   In **Jyeshtha**, the timing is excellent. 
*   In **Ashadha**, it grants success in all undertakings. 
*   In **Shravana**, one is left without wealth. 
*   In **Proshthapada** <note>original: "proshthapada"; another name for the month of Bhadrapada</note>, one becomes sorrowful. 
*   In **Ashwayuja** <note>the month of Ashvina</note>, there is the attainment of wealth. 
*   In **Kartika**, there is danger from fire. 
*   In **Saumya** <note>the month of Margashirsha</note>, matchless good fortune is found. 
*   In **Pausha**, there is nourishment and supreme growth. 

Regarding the days of the week, a work started on a **Sunday** becomes radiant and energetic, while on a **Monday**, it brings security and peace. These calculations are said to bring joy to the world when measured correctly. The Sun's influence at the start and the stable influence of Saturn are traditionally cited for a firm installation.

### Now, the Installation of Deities

The **Pratishtha** <note>the ritual of "establishing" or consecrating a deity's image in a temple</note> should take place under the star **Pushya**, or the "gentle" stars <note>the Mridu Nakshatras: Mrigashira, Chitra, Anuradha, and Revati</note>, or under **Vaishvadeva** <note>the star Uttara Ashadha</note>. Installation under the influence of the wind <note>the star Swati</note> is also praised for commemorative rites. 

The installation is said to be radiant, though a "slender fire" or the influence of Rahu may cause obstacles. It brings joy, welfare, and a dwelling of prosperity. When performing the installation on various days:
*   **Sun in Leo:** Auspicious for general installations.
*   **Sun in Gemini:** Auspicious for the installation of **Maheshwara** <note>Lord Shiva</note>.
*   **Sun in Capricorn:** Auspicious for **Narayana** <note>Lord Vishnu</note>.
*   **Sun in [unclear]:** Auspicious for the Creator, **Brahma**.

The deity should be placed within the dwellings of the worlds when the planets are stable. The installation is "unshakeable" when performed during the bright fortnight <note>Shukla Paksha; the waxing moon</note>, in the lunar portion, and when Jupiter's influence is in the **Lagna** <term>Lagna|The Ascendant, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>. Benefic planets should be situated in the **Kendra** <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart, considered the pillars of strength</term>, the ninth house, or the house of religion, while malefic planets should be placed in the sixth house. The installation of gods is best when the moon is in the "gentle" stars, or in **Pushya**, or divided <unclear>in thought</unclear>.

### Now, the Consideration of Setting Out

For a journey or "setting out" (**Prasthana**), the stars **Mrigashira**, **Tishya** <note>Pushya</note>, **Anuradha**, **Ashwini**, **Shravana**, and **Revati** are always beautiful and auspicious. 

Specifically, **Ashwini**, **Maitra** <note>Anuradha</note>, **Revati**, **Mrigashira**, **Mula**, **Punarvasu**, **Tishya**, **Hasta**, and **Jyeshtha** are preferred for a departure. **Rohini** and the three **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>...

<keywords>Vastu Shastra, Pratishtha, Muhurta, Lunar Months, Deva Pratishtha, Shiva, Vishnu, Prasthana, Astrological Timing</keywords>

Setting out during the stars **Hasta, Uttaraphalguni, Shatabhisha** <note>original: "varunau", referring to the deity Varuna who rules Shatabhisha</note>, **Shravana, Rohini** <note>original: "vidhi", referring to the creator Brahma who rules Rohini</note>, and **Dhanishtha** <note>original: "nishtha"</note> yields a medium result. However, traveling during **Vishakha, Uttarabhadrapada**, **Ardra**, and **Bharani**, or **Ashlesha** and **Krittika**, brings the threat of death. Under **Krittika, Vishakha, Magha, Ardra,** or **Bharani**, one should never set out, for there is no returning home.

### Thus, the Constellations for Setting Out

On a **Monday** or **Wednesday** <note>original: "somakajair", referring to the Moon (Soma) and Mercury (the son of the Moon)</note>, one should travel South for gain. On a **Thursday**, traveling West leads to the successful completion of work. On a **Friday** or **Sunday**, travel North for the gain of grain. On a **Tuesday**, travel East for the gain of wealth and grain. [The text describes the planetary lords of the directions: Southeast, Southwest, Northwest]. If one sets out during the influence of the wrong planetary lord, there is no return. Traveling East, Southeast, or South [under specific influences] or toward the South, Southwest, or West when Jupiter is poorly placed, or toward the West or Northwest during Mercury’s influence, or toward the North during Mars’ influence, leads to certain death and no success.

### Now, the Consideration of Ominous Time

Avoid traveling in the following directions <note>a practice known as "dishashula" or the "spike of the directions"</note> on these specific days: on a **Sunday**, avoid the South; on a **Monday**, avoid the Southwest; on a **Tuesday**, avoid the West; on a **Wednesday**, avoid the Northwest; on a **Thursday**, avoid the North; on a **Friday**, avoid the Southeast; and on a **Saturday**, avoid the East. Following these rules leads to the gain of a kingdom; however, traveling in the forbidden direction leads to death—of this there is no doubt.

### Now, the Consideration of Lunar Days

The first lunar day (**Pratipada**) is blessed for travel; the second (**Dwitiya**) brings security; the third (**Tritiya**) leads to the gain of land; the fourth (**Chaturthi**) brings security; and the fifth (**Panchami**) leads to the loss of wealth...

<meta>This page continues the astrological instructions for travel (yatra) from the previous page, specifically focusing on the auspiciousness of lunar days (tithis) and the position of the Yoginis—directional goddesses whose location determines the outcome of a journey or battle.</meta>

On the seventh day, there is quarreling, yet it is also the **"Victory Seventh"** <note>original: "vijaya-saptami"</note>, abundant in wealth and grain. On the eighth day, illness increases. On the ninth, one faces obstructions <note>original: "vedhanam," literally a piercing or wounding</note>.

On the tenth, wealth is granted daily. On the eleventh, victory comes. One must never set out on the twelfth. All success is found on the thirteenth. Whether during the bright or dark fortnight, the fourteenth should be avoided. On the fifteenth—in either fortnight—one must not set out. These are the rules for departure according to the <term>tithis|The 30 divisions of a lunar month; lunar days</term>, as specified by the followers of **Rudra**.

### Now, the Yoginis

On the first and ninth days, **Brahmani** resides in the East.
On the second and tenth, **Maheshvari** is in the North.
On the third and eleventh, **Kaumari** is in the Southeast <note>original: "agnikonā," the corner of the fire-god Agni</note>.
On the fourth and twelfth, **Vaishnavi** is in the Southwest <note>original: "naiṛtorayā," the corner of the demon Nairṛti</note>.
On the fifth and thirteenth, **Varahi** is in the South <note>original: "rākṣasīrayā," the direction of the demons</note>.
On the sixth and fourteenth, **Indrani** is in the West.
On the seventh and the New Moon <note>original: "māvasyāṃ," the dark moon</note>, **Chamunda** is in the Northwest <note>original: "vāyavyakoṇāḥ," the corner of the wind-god Vayu</note>.
On the eighth and the Full Moon, **Mahalakshmi** resides in the Northeast <note>original: "śivālayā," the abode of Shiva</note>.

If the <term>Yogini|A group of eight powerful goddesses who move through the compass points; their position relative to a traveler determines success or failure</term> is on one's left, the task is accomplished. If she is on the right, there is great fear. If one faces her directly, they meet death. If she is behind <note>original: "pashchime," literally 'at the back' or 'West'</note>, victory and gain are achieved. 

One must avoid the direction where the goddess stands in front. In battle, travel, gambling, and various affairs, the position of the Yogini should always be contemplated with great effort. 

Under the influence of Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus <note>original: "śrīpate-guru-saumya-bhāgaurite"</note>, and when the <term>Lagna|The rising sign or ascending zodiac degree at the time of departure</term> and the moon sign are favorable, the journey set out upon will be auspicious. When the stars are favorable and the Sun is pleased, traveling while provided with horses and elephants brings happiness and victory. One should set out for victory.

...which should always be held in the mind of the king. When the benefic planets <note>original: "shubha"</note> are situated in the <term>kendras|The angular houses of an astrological chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th)</term> and the <term>trikonas|The trine houses (1st, 5th, and 9th)</term>, and the malefic planets <note>original: "papa"</note> are excluded from the <term>kendras</term> and the eighth house, it leads to the supreme accomplishment of all goals for men. If the positions are reversed, the results shall be the opposite.

A Brahmin, a cow, a full pitcher <note>original: "purna-kumbha"</note>, curd, fruit, flowers, wine, a courtesan, milk with her calf, a householder, a king, meat, cooked rice, wealth, a vehicle, a full vessel, a water-creature (fish), a bird, a perfected mantra, and a woman in beautiful clothing—if these are seen or heard at the time of departure, they are considered excellent.

One anointed with oil, a king of snakes, one with loose hair, a naked person, a person in hiding, one with disheveled hair, and those with empty hands—if these are encountered by those setting out, they lead to a forced return, death, obstacles, destruction, or separation. A fox, a wandering fire-flame, or a washerman are not auspicious for those departing. One should listen for the cry of a crow and then proceed according to its direction. By dividing the omens into six parts, the results are determined: profit, fear, happiness, or a meeting at a meal. If there is no remainder <note>referring to a mathematical calculation based on the omen</note>, death is foretold. These are the characteristics of crow-omens <note>original: "kaka-lakshanam"</note>.

## This concludes the rules for travel.

## Now, the Initiation Ceremony <note>original: "Upanayana"</note>.

When the sun is in its northward path <term>Uttarayana|The six-month period between the winter and summer solstices, considered spiritually auspicious</term>, during the bright fortnight <note>original: "shukla-paksha"</note>, on an auspicious day, one should avoid the "empty" lunar days <note>original: "rikta," the 4th, 9th, and 14th days</note>, as well as the sixth, eighth, and fifth days. 

In the spring, a **Brahmin** should be initiated; in the summer, a **Kshatriya** <note>original: "rajan," the warrior-kingly caste</note> is recommended; in the autumn, a **Vaishya**; and in the winter, those of the **Shudra** caste <note>The inclusion of Shudras in the initiation ritual here is a notable regional or period-specific variation from stricter Vedic traditions</note>.

In the eighth year from conception is the thread ceremony <term>Maunjibandhana|The ritual of tying the sacred belt made of Munja grass</term> for Brahmins. For a **Vaishya**, it is in the eleventh year. The limit for a **Brahmin** must not exceed the sixteenth year. For a **Kshatriya**, the limit is between seventeen and twenty-two, and for a **Vaishya**, it is twenty-four years.

### The Auspicious Timing for the Girdle Ceremony

In the seasons of **late winter** <note>original: "shishira"</note>, **summer** <note>original: "grishma"</note>, **spring** <note>original: "vasanta"</note>, and also in the **autumn** <note>original: "sharad"</note>, the auspicious rite of the <term>Munja girdle|A belt made of sacred Munja grass tied around the waist of the student during initiation</term> should be performed. One must ensure that the Moon and the stars <note>original: "chandra-tara"</note> are favorable; if they are not, the ceremony should be avoided. For the twice-born classes <note>original: "dvijatis"; referring to the three upper social classes</note>, the positions of Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, and the Moon are of the highest importance at this time to ensure the ceremony is fruitful <note>original: "pavati" likely a scribal variant for "bhavati"</note>. The physical health of the boy and the accumulation of spiritual merit are considered by the kingly sages as the foundation for this ritual.

### The Influence of the Ascendant

The following results are foretold based on the <term>Lagna|The Ascendant, or the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ceremony</term> at the moment of initiation:

*   In **Aries** <note>original: "mesha"</note>: The Brahmin becomes a master of speech.
*   In **Taurus** <note>original: "vrisha"</note>: O best of sages, one becomes destitute and lacking in wealth.
*   In **Cancer** <note>original: "karkata"</note>: One finds their proper livelihood and disposition.
*   In **Leo** <note>original: "simha"</note>: One becomes a conqueror of enemies.
*   In **Virgo** <note>original: "kanya"</note> or **Libra** <note>original: "tula"</note>: One attains **spiritual splendor** <note>original: "brahma-varchas"</note>.
*   In **Scorpio** <note>original: "vrishchika"</note>: One is unfortunately inclined toward thievery.
*   In **Capricorn** <note>original: "makara"</note>: One remains constantly on the move.
*   In **Pisces** <note>original: "meena"</note>: The Brahmin is devoted to worldly knowledge but may be excluded from Vedic practice.

### Rules for the Rite

For the **initiation ceremony** <note>original: "maunjibandhanam"</note>, the wise should ensure the student faces the **East**. For kings and merchants, the placement of Mercury <note>original: "soma-suta"</note> is particularly vital. In all cases of divine work, facing East is honored by the gods. 

One must avoid the time when **Venus** is in the third house. Jupiter should be in a strong triple-strength position and must not be in the house of its decline. One should avoid Mars and Saturn during the crucial moments of the ritual. The Moon should be full and increasing in strength <note>original: "shukla-paksha"</note>, and the planets must be in auspicious houses—specifically the fifth, ninth, or the <unclear>angular houses</unclear>—along with Venus...

### Auspicious Planets for the Social Classes

Mercury and Jupiter are considered auspicious for all signs, while Venus and Mercury are specifically desired for the rites of the **Priestly class** <note>original: "viprāṇāṃ"; the Brahmins</note>. The Sun and Mars are praised for the **Warrior class** <note>original: "kṣatriyāṇāṃ"</note> to ensure a successful outcome. For the **Merchant class** <note>original: "vaiśyāṇāṃ"</note>, the Moon is said to be favorable, and the Moon is also beneficial for the **Laboring class** <note>original: "śūdrayoḥ"</note>.

### Results of the Months for Initiation

The following results are foretold based on the <term>Lunar Month|The Hindu calendar months based on the moon's cycle</term> in which the initiation occurs:

*   **Magha (January-February):** One becomes extremely wealthy, a master of riches <note>original: "dhanapati"</note>.
*   **Phalguna (February-March):** One becomes endowed with great wisdom and intelligence.
*   **Chaitra (March-April):** This month results in emptiness or lack <note>original: "rikti"</note>.
*   **Vaishakha (April-May):** One is accompanied by the enjoyment of great pleasures.
*   **Jyeshtha (May-June):** The wise student attains a very long life.
*   **Ashadha (June-July):** One achieves mastery over the great treatises of the scriptures and gains high status through wisdom.
*   **Sravana and Bhadrapada (July-September):** One becomes sorrowful, excluded from religious duty <note>original: "dharma"</note>, and lacks proper conduct.
*   **Kartika (October-November):** One is always accompanied by happiness and many children.
*   **Margashirsha (November-December):** This month creates various forms of happiness.
*   **Pausha (December-January):** One is blessed with children.

### The Coronation of Kings

**Now, the procedure for the Royal Consecration** <note>original: "atha rājyābhiṣekam"</note>.

The coronation should be performed under the following <term>Lunar Mansions|Nakshatras, or specific stars the moon passes through</term>: **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitra"</note>, **Hasta** <note>original: "arka-kara"</note>, **Rohini**, **Chitra**, **Revati**, and **Mrigashira**. These bring honor to the names of the King's father and mother. 

One must be cautious of the planetary positions: 
*   If **malefic planets** are in the house of wealth, destruction of wealth will occur. 
*   If they are in the ninth house (religion), the King will constantly commit sins. 
*   If in the tenth house (action), he will be lazy. 
*   If in the first house (the self), he will lack shelter and have a short life.

### The Ritual for Establishing Sacred Fires

**Now, the method for the Establishment of the Sacred Fires for Vedic Scholars** <note>original: "atha śrotriyāṇām agnyādhāna-vidhiḥ"</note>.

The <term>Three Sacred Fires|The Trikagni (Ahavaniya, Garhapatya, and Dakshina) maintained by a householder</term> should be established under the following stars: **Rohini** <note>original: "prājāpatye"</note>, **Revati** <note>original: "pauṣṇabhe"</note>, **Ashlesha** <note>original: "sārpam"</note>, **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitre"</note>, **Pushya**, **Dhanishtha** <note>original: "vāsava"</note>, **Hasta**, and the three **Latter stars** <note>original: "uttarāṇāṃ traya"; referring to Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>. This has been declared by the ancient sages. The ritual is particularly auspicious when the month of **Chaitra** arrives, provided Mercury is in a favorable position. <margin>12</margin>
