<meta>This page transitions from the rules for establishing sacred fires and planetary influences on rituals to the field of "Jyotish-Chikitsa" (Medical Astrology). It specifically details the "Jvara-vicāra" or the inquiry into fevers, providing a prognosis for recovery or danger based on the specific Lunar Mansion (Nakshatra) occupied by the moon at the onset of the illness.</meta>

Even now, one who travels without the light of knowledge becomes an object of ridicule. If the planets are unfavorable, the ritual remains fruitless. However, if the benefics—Venus, Mercury, and Jupiter—are placed in the seventh house, the individual grows without fault. Without such wealth of planetary support, one is devoid of a long life and moves toward death. At the time of a crisis in the seventh house, one is stripped of prosperity. For one whose **Commencement of the Sacred Fire Ritual** <note>original: "agnihotra-prārambhe"</note> occurs while a malefic planet is present, failure follows. However, when benefics occupy the center houses <term>Kendra|The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart, considered the pillars of a horoscope</term>, success is born for all men.

### Now, the Inquiry into Medical Treatment
<note>original: "Cikitsā-vicāraḥ"</note>

A **Fever** <note>original: "jvara"</note> that begins under the mansion of **Ashwini** ceases quickly through known remedies like the "sword-edge" <note>This likely refers to a specific potent medicinal preparation or a ritualistic cleansing</note>. If it begins under **Bharani**, it lasts for five nights. Under **Rohini**, it may last for three nights or eight nights. Under **Mrigashira**, the duration is eleven days, or sometimes double that for each specific case. 

After five days or nights, or perhaps on the thirteenth day, the fever follows its course. In **Punarvasu**, the fever lasts for three days, or sometimes for a period of two or three days depending on the strength of the patient. Under **Pushya**, if the fever begins at the rising of the sign, there is a risk of death if it is not resolved by the twelfth day. In the two **Phalguni** mansions <note>Purva-Phalguni and Uttara-Phalguni</note>, health returns after twelve days of fever. 

Under **Hasta**, peace is established on the seventh day, or perhaps the eighth. If the fever continues to burn, the patient may suffer from delirium for three, eight, or twelve nights. In **Vishakha**, health should be declared on the twenty-second day. Peace comes on the ninth day for those under **Chitra**, and health follows thereafter in the mansion of **Anuradha** <note>original: "maitra"; the mansion ruled by the deity Mitra</note>. 

In **Jyeshtha**, health returns on the fifth, ninth, or twelfth day. In **Mula**, one may suffer from an irregular or chronic fever <note>original: "viṣamam"; likely referring to Vishama-jvara, often associated with malaria or recurring fevers</note>. Under **Purvashadha**, the fever lasts for six days. Under **Shravana**, intense ill-health persists for nine or twelve days. In **Dhanishtha**, if health does not return by the tenth day, the condition is grave.

## Now, the Constellations for the Commencement of Education
<note>original: "vidyārambha-nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

The constellations of **Hasta**, **Mrigashira**, **Shravana**, **Ashwini**, **Swati**, **Rohini**, and **Anuradha** are excellent for the acquisition of knowledge. Among the planets, **Jupiter** <note>original: "guru"</note> is the best for starting education; **Venus** and the **Sun** are considered medium. **Saturn** and **Mars** <note>original: "angaraka"</note> bring "death" to the pursuit of learning, while **Mercury** is always favorable for the intellect.

## Now, the Constellations for the Journey of Cattle
<note>original: "godhuyātrā"</note>

The constellations of **Dhanishtha**, **Shatabhisha**, **Jyeshtha**, and the **Purva** group <note>The "prior" constellations: Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note> are praised for the journey or movement of cattle. Likewise, the constellations of **Anuradha**, **Rohini**, and **Vishakha** are favorable.

## Now, the Constellations for the Giving of Word
<note>original: "vāgdāna-nakṣatrāṇi," referring to a formal betrothal or engagement ceremony</note>

**Ashwini**, **Mrigashira**, **Rohini**, **Swati**, **Vishakha**, **Anuradha**, and **Pushya** are praised as the constellations for the "Giving of the Word" <note>The vāgdāna is the ritual promise of marriage made before the actual wedding</note>. 

## Now, the Birth Constellations of the Planets
<note>original: "graha-nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

The birth mansion of the **Sun** is declared to be **Vishakha**. **Krittika** is the mansion of the **Moon**, and **Swati** is for **Mars**. **Dhanishtha** belongs to **Mercury** (the son of the Moon), and **Purva Phalguni** is for **Jupiter**. For **Venus**, the birth mansion is **Revati**. **Bharani** is the mansion of **Rahu**, and **Ashlesha** is said to be for **Ketu**. **Ashwini** is for **Agastya**, and **Shravana** is for the **Pole Star** <note>original: "dhruva"</note>. These are known as the primary mansions of the planets.

## Now, the Constellations for Childbirth
<note>original: "prasava-nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

In **Hasta**, **Mula**, **Punarvasu**, **Shravana**, and **Mrigashira**, a child is born with success and brings prosperity to the lineage.

## Now, the Three Auspicious Ascendants
<note>original: "golagna-trayam"</note>

The moment of **Abhijit**, the time of "Nectar," and the time of the "Dust of the Cows" <note>original: "godhūli," the auspicious period of twilight when cattle return home</note> are the three great <term>Lagnas|The specific zodiac sign rising on the horizon; used here to describe an auspicious window of time</term> in which one should perform all important tasks. At dawn, when the network of rays breaks through the morning mist...

...the pride of the mountains is destroyed, and the wise man achieves success in the midst of his kinsmen upon the king's return. At the time of dawn, the **Cattle-Ascendant** <note>original: "go-lagna"; an auspicious time associated with the rising sun and the waking of cattle</note> occurs. 

The **Abhijit** <note>The "Victorious" period; a specific auspicious window of time around midday</note> is excellent for all tasks at all times. It is liberated from all astrological flaws and avoids the defects caused by the planets. In the day, at midday, it lasts for approximately twenty-four minutes <note>original: "ghatikā-ekā," though traditionally a ghatika is 24 minutes, Abhijit is often calculated as the mid-portion of the day</note>. At that time, when the sun is at the meridian, even if there is some slight movement, no fault occurs. This concludes the **Cattle-Ascendant** at midday.

The dust raised by the hooves of the cows and the feet of the Brahmins <note>original: "go-bhish-cha brāhmaṇānāṃ ca khura-puṭṭa-dalitā-yātu dhūlir"</note> brings great wealth, children, long life, health, and prosperity. At that specific time, there is no need to consider the constellation, the lunar day, the half-day, the ascendant, or the planetary combinations. It is known among men as the time that destroys all sins and prevents the downfall of kings.

### On the Analysis of the Malignant Conduct and Retrograde Characteristics of Mars
<note>original: "atha bhaumasya hīnācāra vicāra vakra-lakṣaṇa"</note>

When **Mars** <note>original: "bhauma"</note> moves in a lowly manner or becomes **retrograde** <note>original: "vakri"; appearing to move backward in the sky</note>, there is a great fear of fire, fevers, and excessive colic pain. There is also the fear of dysentery, distress among cattle, and the shaking of mountains, yet it does not result in rainfall. This concludes the section on lowly movement. 

When the **Sun**, **Moon**, and **Mars** <note>original: "divākara-indu-staraja-kuja"</note> are in certain placements, they provide strength; however, if they are situated in the house of loss <note>the 12th house</note>, even if they appear beautiful, they may cause death. When the **Sun** and other planets are in conjunction, they oppress one another; if influenced by malefic planets, the result is sorrow.

### On the Analysis of the Combustion of Jupiter
<note>original: "athālaṃ gata prakaśi vṛhaspati vicāraḥ"</note>

When **Venus** <note>original: "daitya-guru"; the teacher of the demons</note> and **Jupiter** <note>original: "jīva"</note> are **combust** <note>original: "astāṃ gata"; too close to the sun to be seen</note>, there is much conflict among the lords of men. **Vetalas** <note>spirit-beings or vampires</note> and **Yakshas** <note>nature spirits</note> suddenly become intoxicated or maddened, and there is the destruction of clothing and fire-related distress.

### On the Analysis of Saturn
<note>original: "atha śanaiścara vicāraḥ"</note>

As long as it is **direct** <note>original: "mārgī"; moving in its normal forward direction</note>, it increases the strength of the body and the house of wealth... in the middle of **Pisces** <note>original: "mīna"</note>...

At the end of the sign of Virgo <note>original: "kanyānta"</note>, as many young people as there are [degrees], there shall be the death of the masterless and great thirst among the subjects. Due to the expansion of the earth and the rising of waters, there is a sequence of fire and logic-defying events; through the dragging of the plow <note>original: "karṣaṇaiḥ"</note>, wealth, fire, and the abundance of fruits are affected. There is opposition among the "holders of the earth" <note>original: "dharaṇidhara"; referring either to mountains or kings</note> and the torment of disease among the subjects. The death of the young, trembling of the earth, and the fall of lightning or heat-waves occur. An irregular seasonal combination leads to the destruction of crops everywhere. When the Sun is devoid of its luster, it signifies a danger of thieves for the people. In battle, death comes to the subjects, and there is an unnatural change in the behavior of cattle. The great and the wise constantly wander the earth while crops are destroyed. Even for the high-born and the kings of kings, there is ruin in **Kashmir** <note>A historic region in the northern Indian subcontinent, often a focal point for these specific astrological manuscripts</note> due to the rapid transit <note>original: "aticāra"; an abnormally fast movement of a planet through a zodiac sign</note> of **Jupiter** <note>original: "vṛhaspati"</note>.

### The Scissor Combination
<note>original: "Kartari Yoga"; an astrological configuration where a house or planet is "pinched" or hemmed in between two malefic planets, like the blades of scissors</note>

The **Pillar-Scissor** <note>original: "Stambha-kartari"</note> combination involves women; the **Arrow-Scissor** <note>original: "Bhāna-kartari"</note> is caused by **Mars** <note>original: "Bhūmija"; literally "born of the earth"</note>. The **Time-Scissor** <note>original: "Kāla-kartari"</note> is formed by the **Moon** <note>original: "Soma"</note> with others. In the **Destruction-Scissor** <note>original: "Saṃhāra-kartari"</note>, monkeys enter the homes of the residents of **Kashmir**. The division of the kingdom and the yoga of the king are thus described.

### Analysis of the Lord of the Ganas
<note>original: "Gaṇapāṇeśvara-vicāra"; "Gana" refers to groups or attendants; this likely refers to a presiding deity or a specific calculation for determining the fate of the masses</note>

Through the groups of malefic planets <note>original: "krūra-graha"</note>, the wealth of the people and the world shall be destroyed by fire. The uprooting of kingdoms occurs in a short time, accompanied by the fall of meteors and famine. On the seventh bank, the cattle die. From the movement in the sixth and third houses, there is the torment of disease, specifically bile and water-related afflictions <note>original: "pitta-jala"</note>. The country faces extreme discord, and the low-born become widespread. Though there may be prosperity and the filling of the oceans with water, there is still fear. The gods suffer a loss of power, and pain comes through the lords of the elephants and serpents. The entire nation and the middle of the cities are burdened by the fall of the country's leaders.

### Characteristics of the Year
<note>original: "Varṣa-lakṣaṇa"; specifically referring to the omens for rainfall and the agricultural cycle of the upcoming year</note>

Salutation to the Illustrious Ganesha. One should contemplate the **Hand** <note>original: "Hasta"; the 13th Nakshatra or lunar mansion</note> and the constellation of **Tvashtri** <note>original: "Tvāṣṭre"; referring to the Chitra Nakshatra, presided over by the celestial architect Tvashtri</note>...

...in the mansion of the Wind <note>Shatabhisha</note>, the Friend <note>Anuradha</note>, or Revati, Mars <note>original: "bhīma"</note> is said to behave thus. **Now, the constellations for wells**
<note>original: "kūpanakṣatrāṇi"; specific lunar mansions considered auspicious for digging wells to ensure a steady water supply</note>

The mansions of Hasta, Magha, Anuradha, the three "Utara" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Rohini, Shatabhisha <note>original: "vāruṇa"</note>, and Revati are praised for the digging of wells.

**Now, the constellations for churning**
<note>original: "manthana nakṣatrāṇi"; auspicious times for churning butter or medicinal preparations</note>

Ardra, Magha, Bharani, the three "Purva" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Mula, Shatabhisha, and Ashlesha <note>original: "ahī"</note> are the nine constellations prescribed for churning on earth.

**Now, the Tripushkara constellations**
<note>original: "tripuṣkara"; a "Triple-Lotus" astrological combination believed to triple the outcome of any event—good or bad—occurring during its influence</note>

Punarvasu, Anuradha, Krittika, Uttaraphalguni, and likewise Bhadra and Vishakha—these are known as the **Tripushkara** combinations. When the second, seventh, or twelfth lunar days <note>Tithis</note> coincide respectively with Tuesday <note>Mars</note>, Sunday <note>the Sun</note>, or Thursday <note>Jupiter</note>, through this yoga <note>alignment</note>, everything becomes threefold in its effect.

**Now, the Dhanishtha Five**
<note>original: "dhaniṣṭhā-pañcakaṃ"; a period of five lunar mansions starting from Dhanishtha that is considered highly inauspicious for certain activities, especially funerals, as it is feared to cause further deaths in the family</note>

The mansions of Dhanishtha <note>original: "vāsava"</note>, Shatabhisha <note>original: "vārauna"</note>, Purvabhadrapada <note>original: "ajaikapāta"</note>, Uttarabhadrapada <note>original: "ahirbudhnya"</note>, and Revati are known as the **Dhanishtha Five**. When one must act during the Dhanishtha Five, a ritual of fivefold expansion <note>prapañcaka</note> must be performed: using gold, a figurine <note>heru</note>, and five gems of great luster. For three Brahmins, one should provide food in their bellies, specifically summer-grain, water, and perhaps a ritual doll <note>putraka</note> to ward off the omen.

**Now, the constellations for planting trees**
<note>original: "vṛkṣāropaṇa-nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

Vishakha, Ashvini, the mansion of the Sun <note>original: "sāvitra," referring to the constellation Hasta</note>, Mula, Pushya <note>original: "tiṣya"</note>, Mrigashira, and Shravana are considered auspicious constellations for the planting of trees.

**Now, the auspicious constellations for sowing seeds**
<note>original: "gomangala-bījavāpana-nakṣatrāṇi"</note>

If seeds are sown while the sun is in Aries <note>original: "meṣe"</note>, there is a fear of the government or the king regarding the grain. When seeds are sown in Sagittarius <note>original: "dhanu"</note>, it brings distress to the righteous. Sowing in Leo <note>original: "siṃhe"</note> is said to be destructive, while in Virgo <note>original: "kanyāyāṃ"</note>, it results in a burning or blighted crop. In Libra <note>original: "tulena"</note>, the grain shines only for a moment. In Scorpio <note>original: "vṛścikena"</note>, however, the grain flourishes. When seeds are sown in Capricorn <note>original: "makara"</note>, Pisces <note>original: "mīne"</note>, Gemini <note>original: "mithuna"</note>, or Cancer <note>original: "karki"</note>, the grain grows without any affliction or calamity.

There are eight stages of growth which represent the agricultural wealth of the earth:
1. First, the auspicious beginning <note>original: "maṅgalam"</note>.
2. Second, the sowing of the seed <note>original: "bījavāpana"</note>.
3. Third, the sprouting <note>original: "aṅkura"</note>.
4. Fourth, the formation of the internal embryo <note>original: "garbha-sambhava"</note>.
5. Fifth, the solidifying of the embryo <note>original: "garbha-saṅghāta"</note>.
6. Sixth, the ripening <note>original: "āthāyaṇa" likely meaning the filling of the grain</note>.
7. Seventh, the maturity of the cluster <note>original: "kalāpa"</note>.
8. Eighth, the arrival of the fruit <note>original: "phala-yoga"</note>.

These eight are the cycles of the earth’s harvest. One should avoid journeys from the village during these times and observe these agricultural requirements.

**Now, the signs of rain**
<note>original: "vṛṣṭi-lakṣaṇam"</note>

When the Sun moves through the three [parts] and water falls from the hands of the clouds, various signs appear. Wise men describe the coming of rain through the appearance of the sky and the movements of planets like Saturn <note>original: "śanaiścara"</note>. If an omen arrives at the beginning of a task, it signifies the loss of work; if in the middle, it signifies the destruction of wealth; and if it appears at the end, it is known to take away life itself. <meta>12</meta>

<margin>

Victory is certain during the "tail of Vishti" <note>Vishti, also known as Bhadra, is an astrological period usually considered inauspicious; however, its "tail" or concluding portion is regarded as a powerful time for success</note>. In the third and fourth [lunar days], and on the tenth, Karapi <note>possibly a specific astrological calculation or ritual timing</note> resides there. This is how it is declared: victory is certain in the tail of Vishti.

On the fourth and eighth lunar days, the lineage is governed by Indra and Agni. In the southern direction, Karapi is spoken of; for men, this relates to the practice of offering water. On the Full Moon day, and the twelfth day, the influence is of the Vasus. The third day is of Varuna <note>god of the oceans</note>, and the fourteenth is of Ishana <note>a form of Shiva</note> in the northeast. The divisions of time consist of seven *ghatikas* <note>a unit of time approximately 24 minutes long</note> each. Five of these seven-fold divisions belong to Hari <note>Vishnu</note>, and ten follow in succession. Therefore, one should place these movements accordingly. These periods are said to dwell in the world of the gods. When these movements occur twice, they are said to dwell in the underworld. When the "Hunter" <note>original: "vyādhaḥ," an astrological affliction</note> is in the world of the gods, death itself stands there. But as long as it dwells in the underworld, everything is auspicious. By following this current sequence, every sign is understood.

**Now, the calculation of the birth-nodes**
<note>original: "atha gaṇanāṃ," referring to the calculation of Gandanta, the perilous junctions between certain constellations</note>

In the final fourth part of Revati and the first part of Ashvini; in the final fourth part of Jyeshtha and the first part of Mula; in the final fourth part of Ashlesha and the first part of Magha <note>these are the three "junction points" between water and fire signs in the zodiac</note>—if born during the day, it destroys the father; if at night, it destroys the mother. If born during the twilight, the person destroys themselves. However, a person born in the middle of these nodes is free from disease. If one survives these junction-points, he will become a king within his family.

In the first division [of these nodes], the mother is destroyed; in the second, the father. In the third, the brother is destroyed; in the fourth, the mother again. In the fifth, it is designated for the father; in the sixth, the destruction of the lineage occurs. In the seventh, it destroys one's own family; in the eighth, the life-force itself. In the ninth, it grants heaven <note>implying death, but with a spiritual reward</note>; and in the tenth, it indicates the attainment of a kingdom.

**Now, the consideration of the Solar Transit**
<note>original: "atha saṅkrānti vicāraḥ," the movement of the sun from one zodiac sign to another</note>

First, the *Karanas* <note>a Karana is half of a Tithi or lunar day; there are 11 types used in Vedic astrology to determine the fitness of a specific time</note>: Bava, Balava, Kaulava, Taitila, Gara, Vanija, and Vishti...

It lists how different phases of these transitions affect family members, ranging from death to the attainment of kingship.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on astrological prognostications, shifting from the specific lunar periods (Vishti) discussed on page 15 to the broader influences of the Sun’s transit (Sankranti) into different signs, and the specific effects based on the day of the week the transit occurs.</meta>

# 16
## Br. Da. Ga.

...favor. Through wakefulness, the "Boar" <note>original: "kola," likely referring to a specific astrological posture or omen</note>, and the dwelling of sleep, the astrological periods of **Bava** and **Balava** <note>two of the eleven "Karanas" or half-lunar days used in Vedic astrology to determine the auspiciousness of an action</note> are established. These bring about the loss of wealth for those who are joined with them, yet for others, they create stability and fearlessness <unclear>....</unclear> otherwise, they bring disease and suffering to the common people. 

The growth of all things, including abundant grain and rice, will be seen, leading to festivals for the king. Even in this time, rain will fall according to the season, and the laws of the kingdom will remain firm. In this particular year, a **favorable transit** <term>Sankranti: the moment the Sun moves from one zodiac sign to another</term> shall arise. However, if the transit is unfavorable, there will be an increase in enemies, conflicts within one's own home, and a "disease of duels" <note>likely referring to civil unrest or social strife</note>. There will be a loss of prosperity in the world.

### The Seated Transit

When the transit is "seated" <note>an astrological classification based on the specific time of day the transit occurs</note>, the world will be swarmed by **thieves and insects**, and there will be great winds and **earthquakes**. One may see deformed births and an absence of medicine. The kings will have "frowning brows" <note>an expression of anger or worry</note> and fickle minds; there will be great fear among them. This transit brings the affliction of dust and the constant threat of thieves. 

### Sun in the Signs of the Fish and the Ram

The hardships previously mentioned will be moderate for those who are thirsty for success. When the transit occurs at the end of the day or at night, it fulfills all purposes. 

*   When the **Sun** enters **Pisces** <note>original: "Ravi meena," the transit marking the solar month of Meena</note>, one should examine the results carefully. 
*   When the transit occurs in **Aries** <note>original: "Mesha"</note> during the bright fortnight or during the day, there will be an abundance of crops. The earth will become truly lush with vegetation.

### Influences of the Planets and Days

The fruits of the Sun's transit are halved if the son of the Earth <note>original: "Mahisuta," a name for the planet Mars</note> is lacking in strength. If the transit coincides with a full moon or a powerful planet, it may be fruitless. At such times, there may be a "rain of weapons" <note>a metaphor for war or violent conflict</note>, anxiety for the king, and loss of rice and grain. While there is a wealth of crops, the subjects may still suffer. 

The effects based on the day of the transit:
*   **Sunday:** If the transit falls on Sunday, there is fear of thieves and enemies.
*   **Monday:** Known as the "water-carrier," it brings coolness and cleansing rains.
*   **Tuesday:** Influenced by Mars, it brings distress from the king and constant restlessness.
*   **Wednesday/Thursday:** Known as the path of the celestial Ganges; when associated with **Jupiter**, it produces an abundance of all crops.

<meta>This page continues the discussion on astrological prognostications, specifically detailing the outcomes of the Sun's transit (Sankranti) based on the day of the week, the zodiac signs involved, and mathematical calculations used to predict economic fluctuations.</meta>

# 6
## Br. Da. Ga.

...[The transit occurring] on **Friday** causes an increase in the death of kings, but through mixed activities, it promotes the welfare of all living beings. On **Saturday**, the transit is known as **Alasi** <note>original: "ālasī," meaning "the Indolent" or "Lazy" one</note>; it is remembered as one that brings suffering to the subjects. The **Transit** <term>Sankranti: the moment the Sun moves from one zodiac sign to another</term> should be analyzed in conjunction with the asterism, the lunar day, the planetary alignments, and the "gates" <note>specific astrological divisions</note>. One should consider these alongside the types of goods and their colors. 

When calculating the remainder <note>likely referring to a common astrological formula where the day's total is divided by a specific number</note>: 
- If **one** remains, there will be an **increase in prices**. 
- If there is a **specific [middle] remainder**, the result is **average**. 
- If there is **no remainder**, there will be a **decrease in prices**. 

One should carefully examine these omens. When the Sun transits through the specific portion of the asterism <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions or star sectors</term> associated with men, one should understand that the specific day of the week becomes irrelevant. In whatever manner the Sun enters a zodiac sign, such shall be the result, whether it be auspicious or inauspicious. 

All the planets are driven by the Sun's strength, just as even women are led by the strength of the Moon. The Moon itself is said to be influenced by the strength of the stars, their conjunctions, and their aspects, which in turn determine the strength and destiny of men.

Regarding the signs of **Pisces, Aries, Virgo, and Cancer**: 
Such a transit in the first year will result in victory over enemies. In the second year, the King or the warrior class <note>original: "kshatriya"</note> shall find a perfectly devoted wife. In the third year, there will be swift destruction by weapons or anger in battle. In the fifth year, there will be constraints or imprisonment. In the sixth, there will be quarreling. In the seventh, there will be a bountiful harvest of grain. In the eighth, there is the risk of self-harm or personal loss. 

In the ninth year, through the observance of rules and restraints, it becomes a creator of righteousness <term>Dharma: the moral and cosmic order</term>. Otherwise, it will bring disease, old age, sickness, and **famine** among the common people.

To destroy all such calamities, there is an increase of prosperity in the seventh [period/sign]. If the transit occurs in **Leo** or **Gemini** and righteousness is maintained, there will be victory. If it exists in **Aries** or **Capricorn**, the people will be oppressed by thieves. 

Therefore, one should perform the worship of the planets for the protection of the wise. The Transit is known by **seven names** <note>these names change depending on the day of the week the transit occurs</note>: 
1. **Manda** (The Slow)
2. **Mandakini** (The Gentle)
3. **Ghora** (The Terrible)
4. **Dhvankshi** (The Raven-like)
5. **Mahodara** (The Big-bellied)
6. **Mishrika** (The Mixed)
7. **Rakshasi** (The Demonic)

<margin>
Br. Da. Ga.
17
</margin>

The **Nanda** transit is beneficial for the **Brahmin** people <term>Brahmin: the priestly and scholarly class</term>. The **Mandakini** <note>original: "mandākinī," often associated with the celestial Ganges</note> as well as the **Dha** <note>possibly a scribal abbreviation for a specific sub-classification</note> are beneficial for the **Vaishya** people <term>Vaishya: the merchant and farmer class</term>. The **Chora** <term>Chora: literally "thief"</term> transits provide auspicious results during mild or gentle times. The **Mahodhari** transit is for the benefit of kings and the **Shonikas** <note>likely referring to the Shonitapura region or a specific warrior lineage</note>. The **Rajasi** <note>referring to the quality of passion or activity</note> transit should be performed for the benefit of the **Chandalas**, **thieves**, and **Mlecchas** <term>Mleccha: a term used for foreigners or those outside the traditional Vedic social structure</term>. For all **four-legged animals**, the **Mishrika** <note>meaning "mixed"</note> transit brings happiness. If the **Transit** <term>Sankranti: the Sun's entry into a new sign</term> occurs at night, its effects are felt on the previous days or the following days respectively. If it occurs in the middle watch of the night, it consumes <note>affects</note> the two days preceding it.

### Now, the Rising of the Kalika Yoga
When **Mars** is in the second house, **Venus** in the third, the **Sun** in the fourth, **Mercury** in the fifth, **Saturn** in the sixth, the **Moon** in the seventh, and **Jupiter** in the eighth, the ill-effects of the **watches** <term>Prahara: a three-hour time division</term> are avoided.

### Now, the Kalika Watch
Five, six, seven, three, seven, four, and eight <note>these numbers likely represent specific mathematical values assigned to the planets or hours</note>; these are called the **Kalikas**. One should avoid the half-watch. When the terrible rays of the planets are encountered: four for **Venus**, six for **Mars**, seven for **Mercury**, eight for the **Moon**, and eight for the middle watch of **Saturn**. These are remembered as the divisions within the sixty [Ghatikas] <note>a Ghatika is 24 minutes</note>... <unclear>dviviṃśāḥ pra prajā myā hātā vite</unclear> these are the Kalikas and the rest.

### Now, the Days of the Serpents
**Vasuki** <note>the King of Serpents</note> is associated with the **Sun** and **Mercury**, where he strikes for two watches. **Pindaraka** is associated with **Mars**. **Chakrahasta** <note>"the one with a wheel in hand"</note> is associated with **Wednesday**. On **Thursday**, it is **Pela**. The **Daitya Naga** <note>"Demon Serpent"</note> is on **Friday**. On **Saturday**, it is **Yatadhana**. **Rahu** <term>Rahu: the shadow planet responsible for eclipses</term> is known to swallow the **Kalika** itself on the day of the **Sun**. On the day of the **Moon**, one should know it is **Vasuki**. On **Tuesday**, know it is **Takshaka**. On **Thursday**, it is **Upaka** and **Karatampa**. On the day of the **Moon** <note>possibly referring to a specific lunar day or a repeat in the cycle</note> in the west, it is **Mahapacha**. On **Friday**, it is the same, and on **Saturday** it is **Yatadhana**. **Rahu** swallows the **Kalika** indeed.

### Now, the Consideration of the Planets
Whatever work a man performs on the days when a planet is in the **eighth house**, it is said to bring good results, even if it be a **malefic planet** <note>original: "pāpa graha," referring to planets like Mars or Saturn</note>. If the **Sun** is in **Pisces** <note>original: "maunai," a variation for Meena/Pisces</note> and the **Moon** resides in the **Hasta** asterism, if it is auspicious...

<summary>This page details the social and occupational impacts of different types of Sun transits (Sankrantis) and outlines the 'Kalika' system of time-division. It also maps specific mythological Naga (serpent) deities to the days of the week for divinatory purposes.</summary>
<keywords>Sankranti, Kalika Yoga, Prahara, Naga Vara, Vasuki, Rahu, planetary houses, astrological timing</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the specific transit names (like Nanda and Mandakini) discussed on the previous page to the "Vara Phala" or the astrological results associated with the specific days of the week. It begins with general agricultural predictions based on planetary positions before providing a detailed list of activities suitable for the days of the Moon (Monday), Mars (Tuesday), Mercury (Wednesday), and Jupiter (Thursday).</meta>

One should avoid all ritual actions when the system is without power or essence <note>original: "niṣkalaṃ," meaning without parts or diminished in strength</note>. When the qualities are balanced and favorable to the mind, one performs all actions without doubt. Wealth and grain are granted. When Mercury and Venus are in the sign of **Virgo** <term>Kanya: the zodiac sign of Virgo</term>, there is an excellent ripening of crops. Grains will be plentiful and of good quality. When Mercury is positioned differently, the crops may be ruined, though water and medicinal herbs will remain abundant.

**The Auspicious Results of the Days of the Week**

The coronation of kings, activities pleasing to royalty, royal duties, and seeking an audience with a king are all favored. On this day, activities concerning honor and respected works will be successful. The planting of trees, matters of beauty and fortune <note>original: "saubhagya," referring to auspiciousness and marital or social prosperity</note>, and the application of ointments or perfumes are beneficial. These are the auspicious works to be done on the day of the **Moon** <term>Soma: Monday</term>.

Furthermore, the joining of things, the piercing of ears, and entering a new house may be performed on the day of the Moon. Likewise, for those who know the rules, the day of **Mars** <term>Bhauma: Tuesday</term> is the time for fire-related works, battles, dealing with hardships, and even for acts of theft or deception. The wise should perform fasts and the planting of "Mars-seeds" <note>likely referring to red-colored crops or plants associated with the heat of Mars</note>. It is an excellent time for matters concerning the mother, or for those seeking the birth of a son.

For long-distance travel, matters of wealth and profit, and for the combination of heavy tasks, one should act on the day of **Mercury** <term>Budha: Wednesday</term>. This is the day for the success of all technical systems <note>original: "tantra"</note>, the making of statues or idols, and the completion of all goals.

On the day of **Jupiter** <term>Guru: Thursday</term>, one should engage in the study of scriptures, the worship of deities, and the building of houses. Performing virtuous deeds, playful leisure, the taking of vows, and the seeking of liberation <note>original: "moksha"</note> are appropriate. One should also trade in grains and ornaments on the day of Jupiter. On the day of **Venus** <term>Shukra: Friday</term>, travel and the training of elephants are highly recommended...

Similarly, the taking of a maiden <note>original: "kanyā-grahaṇa," referring to marriage rituals</note>, buying and selling, sexual union, and the maintenance of others should be performed. The storage of gold <gloss>gold</gloss>, grain, the performance of stable or permanent works, entering a new home, and the tethering of elephants and horses are also favored. One may enter a village or a city. On the day of **Saturn** <term>Shani-dine: Saturday</term>, rituals and works involving iron should be carried out. 

**Now, the results of the planetary positions**

When the **Sun** <term>Bhaskara: the Sun</term> is situated in Pisces, Aries, Taurus, or Cancer, or when it enters Leo, there are riches of wine, clothing, and wealth. One is always adorned with land, wealth, elephants, and various vehicles. When the **Moon** <note>original abbreviation: "caṃ" for Chandra</note> is positioned in Leo, Cancer, or Taurus, a man becomes honored and gains a kingdom. He possesses abundant wealth, is fortunate, achieves great fame, and rejoices in the enjoyments of power and prosperity. 

When **Mars** <term>Kuja/Bhauma: the planet Mars</term> resides in Sagittarius, Pisces, Capricorn, or Leo, or moves into Aries or Scorpio, there is an increase in agriculture <note>original: "phāli," referring to the plowshare</note>, the accumulation of wealth and land, the gain of a kingdom, and the presence of prosperity and cattle. When **Mercury** <term>Budha: the planet Mercury</term> is in Aquarius, Libra, Taurus, Gemini <note>original: "manmatha," a poetic term for Gemini</note>, Virgo, or Aries, a man becomes a worshiper of the gods, the ancestors, and the gurus. He is virtuous, possesses much wealth and fortune, and is a great leader <note>original: "mahārathaḥ," literally 'one who commands a great chariot'</note>. 

When **Jupiter** <term>Guru/Jiva: the planet Jupiter</term> is positioned in Aries, Cancer, Leo, or Scorpio, or is in Sagittarius or Pisces, he obtains happiness and the enjoyment of luxuries, such as the royal parasol, elephants, fine clothes, and immense wealth. When **Venus** <term>Bhargava/Shukra: the planet Venus</term> is situated in Pisces, Aquarius, Gemini, Taurus, or Libra, a person becomes very wealthy. He is a scholar, has many sons, is equal to a king, and possesses gems, enemies' wealth, and many vehicles. 

When **Saturn** <term>Shani: the planet Saturn</term> is in Aquarius, Libra, Taurus, Gemini, or Virgo, he causes the fulfillment of desires. There is the gain of servants, wealth, good iron, daughters, land, gold, cows, and buffaloes.

**Now, the consideration of the harsh year, lunar dates, and constellations**

One should abandon the sixth lunar day, and on the seventh, one should avoid a period of five days. [Inauspicious results occur] when the lunar dates are diminished by five and the constellations by seven... <note>This section describes complex mathematical subtractions used to identify "void" or "harsh" timings for travel or rituals</note>.

## Now, the Timing of Cosmic Cycles ##

On the fifteenth day of the dark fortnight of the month of **Magha** <term>January/February</term>; on the thirteenth day of **Nabhas** <term>the month of Bhadrapada, August/September</term>; on the third day of the bright fortnight of **Madhava** <term>the month of Vaishakha, April/May</term>; and on the ninth day of the month of **Urja** <term>the month of Kartika, October/November</term>—these are the **Yugadis** <note>original: "yugādayah," the anniversaries of the start of the four Great Ages of the world</note>.

When the **Sun** <term>Bhanu</term> enters the zodiac signs of Leo, Scorpio, Aries, and Capricorn, that moment is known as the **Yuganta** <note>the "end of an age"</note>. Whatever offerings are made to learned priests or cast into the sacrificial fires at the time of a Yuganta will satisfy one's ancestors for thousands of millions of **Kalpas** <note>A Kalpa is a "Day of Brahma," lasting 4.32 billion years</note>. On the dark fortnight of **Ashadha** <term>June/July</term> and the bright fortnight of **Pausha** <term>December/January</term>, one who performs these rites reaches the world of **Vishnu**.

## Now, the Shravana Twelfth ##

When the twelfth lunar day <term>Dwadashi</term> of the bright fortnight of the month of **Nabhas** <term>Bhadrapada</term> coincides with a Wednesday and the **Shravana** constellation <note>the "Ear" asterism associated with Vishnu</note>, the Lord **Hari** <term>Vishnu</term> is said to be personally present. This specific alignment of **Shravana Dwadashi** is rare even for the gods to encounter. Whatever is given in charity or offered into the fire by men at this time washes away all their sins.

## Now, the Dashahara ##

In the month of **Jyeshtha** <term>May/June</term>, during the bright fortnight, on the tenth day <term>Dashami</term>, when the stars and planets align in the joyful "Ashvaka" formation—this timing is rare even at **Gangadvara** <note>modern-day Haridwar, where the Ganges enters the plains</note>. Whatever is given, offered, or sacrificed during this time yields a result ten million times greater than usual.

## Now, the Victory Seventh ##

When the seventh lunar day <term>Saptami</term> of a bright fortnight falls on a Sunday and aligns with a "male" constellation, it is called **Vijaya Saptami** <note>the "Seventh of Victory"</note>, which grants a place in the abode of **Brahma**. By bathing in sacred waters on this day, one is never reborn into this world of suffering. It cleanses thousands of impurities and hundreds of sins. Whether committed in secret or openly, even those sins that are "fixed like thunderbolt-paste" <note>original: "vajralepa," referring to permanent or indelible karmic stains</note> are destroyed by the power of the **Vijaya Saptami**.

## Now, the Shadow of the Elephant ##

During the dark fortnight, on the thirteenth day <term>Trayodashi</term>, when the Sun resides in the constellation of **Magha** and the Moon is in **Hasta**, the alignment is known as **Gajacchaya** <note>literally "The Elephant's Shadow"</note>. The merit obtained through ancestral rites <term>Shraddha</term> performed during this alignment is inexhaustible; the fruits of such offerings never diminish.

<summary>This page lists highly auspicious calendar dates (Yugadi, Shravana Dwadashi, etc.) and astronomical alignments that provide extraordinary spiritual merit for charity and ancestral rituals. It emphasizes the power of these specific moments to erase even the most permanent sins and ensure a favorable afterlife.</summary>

<keywords>Yugadi, Yuganta, Shravana Dwadashi, Dashahara, Vijaya Saptami, Gajacchaya, Vishnu, ancestral rites, karma, Hindu calendar</keywords>

### Now, the Multifaceted Results of the Four-Square Alignment ###

### Now, the Consideration of the Zodiac Signs ###

**Aries**, **Taurus**, and the sign of the **Maiden** <term>Virgo</term>—these are described as being like a young girl adorned with jewelry and anklets. Endowed with beauty and a virtuous character, the Maiden is associated with the Moon and the purity of one's lineage. **Leo** is described as being provoked by the gods, yet possessing a form of divine grace. **Sagittarius** is the Archer; regarding the character of the maidens born under these signs, there is no doubt of their excellence.

### Now, the Consideration of the Rohini Constellation ###

If the **Rohini** constellation <note>an auspicious lunar mansion associated with growth and abundance</note> coincides with the dark fortnight of the month of **Shravana** <term>July/August</term> on the tenth lunar day, or even on a "Void" night <note>original: "riktā," days considered traditionally empty of certain powers</note>, and is joined by the **Moon**, the Earth shall overflow with a wealth of crops. When **Jupiter** <term>Vrihaspati</term> is positioned in the house of **Mrigashira** <term>the constellation of the Deer</term>, and the Moon is unobstructed, prosperity will surely follow; of this, there is no doubt.

### Now, the Consideration of Royal Alignments ###

Even if only one planet is positioned in the fifth house <note>the house of intelligence and children</note>, one becomes a king. Such a person is a giver of all good fortune and shall become a **Chakravartin** <insert>and</insert> <note>original: "cakravartī," a universal monarch whose wheels of power roll everywhere</note>. If even a single powerful planet like **Jupiter** <term>Vrihaspati</term> is situated in a "Center" <term>Kendra, the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</term>, it wards off all misfortunes. Indeed, what can all the other planets do to harm someone when Jupiter resides in a central house? By the power of this "Lion's Alignment" <note>Kesari Yoga</note>, the individual attains success, happiness, and a brilliant, charming intellect.

### Now, the Knowledge of Divination ###

Observation of the breath is essential: if the air flows from the right nostril and then exits through the left nostril, it grants the constant fulfillment of one’s daily tasks. By observing the flow of breath between the eastern and western sides <note>referring to the right and left channels of the body</note>, one can know if a project will succeed.

<summary>This page details the auspicious qualities of specific Zodiac signs and the Rohini constellation for agriculture, describes "Royal Alignments" (Raja Yogas) that lead to leadership, and introduces breath-based divination (Svara Shastra).</summary>
<keywords>Aries, Virgo, Rohini, Jupiter, Raja Yoga, Chakravartin, Divination, Breath control, Astrology</keywords>

--- Page 52 ---
<meta>This page continues the manuscript’s technical guidance on astrology and ritual timing. Having previously discussed zodiac signs and royal alignments, the text now transitions into the specific auspiciousness of the "Great Night," the calculation of lunar days (tithis), and the speed at which various planets move through the heavens.</meta>

One should understand that the **Great Night** <note>original: "mahāniśā," the most spiritually potent period of midnight</note> consists of the two middle watches of the night. During this time, the auspiciousness of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> is like being anointed with sandalwood paste. When this time is reached, it is as beautiful as a maiden adorned with garlands. It is the ideal time for weddings, for entering a new home, for the **birth rituals** <term>jātakarmaṇi</term> of children, for the **tonsure ceremony** <term>cūḍākaraṇa</term> <note>the traditional first haircut for a child</note>, and for the activities of kings. Neither the planets nor a stingy man can hinder the growth that comes from divine providence; one should perform these rituals for the highest success.

### Now, the Consideration of the Lunar Day ###

Regarding the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term>, whether it is the vowels or the triplets of sounds that are remembered, whether it is a time of struggle or not, the desire of the practitioner is the seeker of all things.

### Elsewhere Also ###

Whether it is the day of the Sun <note>Sunday</note>, the Moon <note>Monday</note>, or Saturn <note>Saturday</note>, or whether the Lunar Day is at its beginning or end, the results of one's actions are experienced accordingly. Nevertheless, the Lunar Days are to be honored for the success of all purposes.

### Now, the Consideration of <insert>the Motion</insert> of the Planets ###

**Mars** <note>original: "lohitāṅgaḥ," the Red-Bodied One</note> remains in a sign for three fortnights. **Venus** and **Mercury** remain for two fortnights. The **Moon** remains for two nights. For the Sun, the king of the heavens, the stay is half a day per degree. One should know that **Jupiter** <note>original: "guru"</note> stays for more than a month. These planets always occupy the zodiac signs in their respective order. Even if a planet's position is traditionally considered unfavorable for a king, any ill omen is transformed into good fortune if a person possesses the strength of the Moon and the stars. 

If a zodiac sign is afflicted by a "malefic" planet <note>a planet like Mars or Saturn that can indicate challenges</note> in the portion where it resides, the timing is not auspicious. It is said that the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term> possesses one unit of merit. The **Lunar Mansion** <term>Nakshatra</term> is said to be four times as powerful. The **Day of the Week** <term>Vara</term> is said to be eight times as powerful. But everything—all power and success—is ultimately established in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the specific sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of an event</note>. This is the collected wisdom of the science of astronomy and the stars.

<summary>This page details the ritual importance of midnight (Mahanisha) for life-cycle ceremonies and provides a technical breakdown of how long each planet dwells in a zodiac sign, concluding that the Ascendant (Lagna) is the most critical factor in astrological timing.</summary>

<keywords>Mahanisha, Tithi, planetary motion, Lagna, birth rituals, Chudakarana, Mars, Jupiter, astrology</keywords>

...the **Moon** remains in each zodiac sign for two and a quarter days. Thus, this is the essence of **astrology** <term>Jyotisha</term> as declared in the systematic treatises by the best of sages.

### Conclusion ###

Thus ends the **Science of Astrology** <term>Jyotisha Shastra</term> spoken by the venerable **Elder Garga** <note>original: "Vriddha Garga," a legendary sage in Hindu tradition and one of the earliest known authorities on astronomy and omens</note>.

May there be auspiciousness for the entire world. May there be well-being for the **scribe** <term>lekhaka</term>, the **reader** <term>pathaka</term>, and the **listeners** <term>shrotrinam</term>.

Auspiciousness!

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library, Jammu

**No.** 850 - Gha <note>original: ८५० - घ. The letter 'Gha' is a Devanagari character used here as a shelf-mark or classification category to organize the library's holdings.</note>

**Title** The Elder Garga's Treatise
<note>original: वृद्धगार्गी (*Vriddhagargi*). This is an ancient Sanskrit text belonging to the early tradition of Indian astral sciences, detailing astronomical observations, planetary movements, and omens.</note>

**Author** Garga the Elder
<note>original: वृद्धगार्गिः (*Vriddhagargih*). Garga is a legendary sage in Hindu tradition, regarded as one of the fundamental authorities on *Jyotisha* (astral science).</note>

**Extent** 25 **Age** <note>Handwritten '25' indicates the number of folios</note>
<note>The 'Extent' refers to the physical size of the manuscript, specifically that it consists of 25 individual hand-written leaves or folios.</note>

**Subject** Astral Science — Complete
<note>original: ज्योतिषम् - सम्पूर्णम् (*Jyotisham - Sampurnam*). The term *Jyotisha* encompasses both astronomy and astrology in the Indian tradition. 'Complete' signifies that this manuscript contains the entire work from beginning to end.</note>

# Shree Raghunath Sanskrit Research Institute Library,

**No.** 850-Gha <note>original: ८५०-घ. The character 'Gha' is the fourth consonant of the Devanagari alphabet, likely serving as a shelf-mark or category indicator within the library's filing system.</note>

**Title** The Treatise of the Elder Garga <note>original: वृद्धगार्गी (Vriddhagargi). An ancient and influential Sanskrit text focusing on astronomy, natural omens, and the movements of celestial bodies.</note>

**Author** The Elder Garga <note>original: वृद्धगार्गिः (Vriddhagargih). A legendary sage and one of the earliest recognized authorities in the Indian tradition of astral science.</note>

**Extent** 25 [folios] <note>original: २५. This indicates the manuscript consists of twenty-five individual leaves or pages.</note> **Age** ........................

**Subject** Astral Science — Complete <note>original: ज्योतिषम् - सम्पूर्णम् (Jyotisham - Sampurnam). <term>Jyotisha</term> is the traditional Indian system of astronomy and astrology. The term "complete" signifies that this manuscript contains the entire work rather than a fragment.</note>

# The Treatise of the Elder Garga

1. **Om, salutations to the auspicious Ganesha.** <note>original: ॐ श्रीगणेशाय नमः (Om śrī-gaṇeśāya namaḥ). Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity of wisdom and the remover of obstacles, traditionally invoked at the beginning of Hindu manuscripts to ensure the successful completion of the work.</note>

**The Elder Garga said:**

Having bowed down to the Lord of the Universe and to Brahma, the Great Grandfather of all, I shall proclaim the knowledge of Time out of a desire for the welfare of all people. || 1 || <note>original: पितामहम् (Pitāmaham), meaning "Grandfather," is a traditional epithet for the creator god Brahma.</note>

To the inquiring sages, Garga, the most excellent of hermits, explained <term>Astral Science (Jyotisha)</term>—which is the most ancient of all sciences and the means of determining ultimate truth. || 2 || <note>The term Jyotisha refers to the "science of light," encompassing astronomy, mathematics, and the study of celestial influences.</note>

Based on the movements of the planets and the constellations, I shall concisely explain the signs of good and ill fortune for the benefit of the entire world. || 3 ||

In this system, there are seven primary <term>Planets (Grahas)</term> beginning with the Moon; <insert>Rahu</insert> is known as the eighth among them, and <insert>Ketu</insert> is remembered as the ninth. || 4 || <note>In traditional Indian astronomy, the Sun and Moon are classified as planets. Rahu and Ketu are the "shadow planets" or lunar nodes, the points in the sky where the paths of the Sun and Moon intersect, causing eclipses.</note>

<summary>This opening page of the manuscript contains the traditional invocation to Ganesha and introduces the sage Garga, who begins his discourse on the science of time, planetary movements, and their effects on worldly affairs.</summary>

# The Treatise of the Elder Garga

1. **Om, salutations to the auspicious Ganesha.** <note>original: ॐ श्रीगणेशाय नमः (Om śrī-gaṇeśāya namaḥ). Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity of wisdom and the remover of obstacles, traditionally invoked at the beginning of Hindu manuscripts to ensure the successful completion of the work.</note>

**The Elder Garga said:**

Having bowed down to the Lord of the Universe and to Brahma, the Great Grandfather of all, I shall proclaim the knowledge of Time out of a desire for the welfare of all people. || 1 || <note>original: पितामहम् (Pitāmaham), meaning "Grandfather," is a traditional epithet for the creator god Brahma.</note>

To the inquiring sages, Garga, the most excellent of hermits, explained <term>Astral Science (Jyotisha)</term>—which is the most ancient of all sciences and the means of determining ultimate truth. || 2 || <note>The term Jyotisha refers to the "science of light," encompassing astronomy, mathematics, and the study of celestial influences.</note>

Based on the movements of the planets and the constellations, I shall concisely explain the signs of good and ill fortune for the benefit of the entire world. || 3 ||

In this system, there are seven primary <term>Planets (Grahas)</term> beginning with the Moon; <insert>Rahu</insert> is known as the eighth among them, and <insert>Ketu</insert> is remembered as the ninth. || 4 || <note>In traditional Indian astronomy, the Sun and Moon are classified as planets. Rahu and Ketu are the "shadow planets" or lunar nodes, the points in the sky where the paths of the Sun and Moon intersect, causing eclipses.</note>

<summary>This opening page identifies the manuscript as a complete work on astral science by the sage Garga and begins with a traditional invocation to Ganesha and Brahma. It establishes the foundational framework of the text, specifically identifying the nine celestial bodies (Grahas) used in ancient Indian astronomy and astrology.</summary>
<keywords>Garga, Jyotisha, Astrology, Astronomy, Brahma, Ganesha, Planets, Rahu, Ketu, Sanskrit Manuscript, Raghunath Institute</keywords>
